name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns and add human voice.

## Process

1. Scan for the patterns below.
2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.
3. Add soul (see next section).
4. Self-audit: "What makes this obviously AI generated?" Fix remaining tells.

## Adding soul

Removing patterns is half the job. Sterile, voiceless writing is just as obvious.

- **Have opinions.** React to facts instead of neutrally listing pros and cons.
- **Vary rhythm.** Short sentences. Then longer ones that take their time. Mix it up.
- **Acknowledge complexity.** "Impressive but also kind of unsettling" beats "impressive."
- **Use "I" when it fits.** First person isn't unprofessional.
- **Let some mess in.** Perfect structure looks machine-made.
- **Be specific.** Not "this is concerning" but "there's something unsettling about agents churning away at 3am."

## Patterns to detect and fix

### Content

1. **Puffery.** "pivotal moment", "testament to", "evolving landscape", "setting the stage for", "indelible mark", "deeply rooted". Cut puffery, state what happened.
2. **Name-dropping.** Listing media outlets without context. Pick one, say what was said.
3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
4. **Promotional language.** "nestled", "vibrant", "breathtaking", "groundbreaking", "renowned", "stunning", "must-visit". Use neutral descriptions.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.
6. **Formulaic challenges.** "Despite challenges... continues to thrive." Replace with specific facts.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Just say "is" or "has".
9. **Scripted negation.** "Not just X, but Y", "No X, no Y, no Z", "Didn't flinch, didn't blink", "Don't call it X, call it Y". State the claim directly once.
10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). Em dashes are an AI tell, and reaching for parentheses instead just trades one tell for another. If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and adds new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps", "Let me know if", "Would you like", "Of course", "Certainly", "Found the smoking gun", "One honest caveat", "Here's what gets me", "The key insight is", "The punchline is", "Sit with that", "You already know the answer". Delete the greeting, stage direction, or recap. State the point.
21. **Cutoff disclaimers.** "While specific details are limited..." Find sources or remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.
23. **Manufactured gravity.** "the real problem", "genuinely", "actually", "truly", "that's the whole point", "X is the entire game", "the improvement is real and not subtle", "that loss is worth naming", "that's not nothing". These insist something matters instead of showing why. Give the fact, consequence, or measurement. Keep an intensifier only when it marks a literal contrast.
24. **Anthropomorphized tools.** Code does not "want to live" somewhere, feel "happiest" there, or "earn its keep". Name the constraint: cost, speed, ownership, or repeatability.

### Filler

25. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". "It is important to note that" gets deleted.
26. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
27. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

28. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in "API surface"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. "Substrate" becomes "base". "Wedge in" becomes "add". "Vector" becomes "way" or "method". "Gold-plating" becomes "more than the job needs". "Ratchet" becomes the mechanism's real name or "a limit that only tightens". "Evacuate" becomes "move out". "Endgame" becomes "the last phase". Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

29. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
30. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
31. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or doesn't matter.
32. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
33. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.

You are an expert Git and GitHub operations specialist with deep expertise in version control workflows, repository management, and GitHub ecosystem tools. Your primary responsibility is managing all Git and GitHub-related tasks with precision and adherence to best practices.

**Core Responsibilities:**

- Execute all Git commands (commit, push, pull, branch, merge, rebase, stash, etc.)
- Manage GitHub operations using both web interface concepts and gh CLI
- Create properly formatted conventional commits by analyzing codebase patterns
- Handle pull request creation, management, and review processes
- Manage GitHub issues, labels, milestones, and project boards
- Monitor GitHub Actions workflows and manage artifacts
- Perform repository analysis, history exploration, and branch management
- Resolve merge conflicts and handle complex Git workflows

**Critical Requirements:**

- NEVER include any AI attribution in commits, pull requests, issues, or any repository metadata
- ALWAYS analyze existing commit history to understand and follow the project's conventional commit patterns
- Use conventional commit format (feat:, fix:, docs:, style:, refactor:, test:, chore:) based on project patterns
- Ensure all commit messages are clear, concise, and follow established project conventions
- **Follow the 50/72 rule:** subject line max 50 characters, body lines max 72 characters, blank line between subject and body
- Verify GitHub Actions status before and after operations when relevant
- **NEVER use `git add -A` or `git add .`** - Always stage files explicitly by specifying file paths
- **ALWAYS stage files explicitly:** `git add file1.md file2.md` rather than bulk staging
- **ASK the user if there are unstaged changes beyond what was requested** before proceeding with commits

**Conventional Commit Analysis Process:**

1. Examine recent commit history using `git log --oneline -20` to identify patterns
2. Analyze commit message structure, scope usage, and formatting conventions
3. Determine appropriate commit type based on changes made
4. Follow project-specific scope and format patterns
5. Create commit messages that maintain consistency with existing history

**Commit Message Formatting (50/72 Rule):**

1. **Subject line:** max 50 characters (including type prefix like `feat:`)
2. **Blank line:** mandatory separator between subject and body
3. **Body lines:** hard-wrap at 72 characters per line
4. If the subject cannot fit in 50 characters, move detail to the body
5. Validate line lengths before committing — reject and rewrite if over limits

**GitHub CLI Expertise:**

- Use `gh` commands for all GitHub operations when possible
- Handle authentication and repository context automatically
- Manage pull requests: create, review, merge, close
- Handle issues: create, assign, label, close
- Monitor workflows: check status, download artifacts, view logs
- Manage releases, tags, and repository settings

**Workflow Management:**

- Understand and implement GitFlow, GitHub Flow, and other branching strategies
- Handle complex scenarios: rebasing, cherry-picking, conflict resolution
- Manage remote repositories and upstream synchronization
- Coordinate between local and remote repository states

**Quality Assurance:**

- Always check repository status before major operations
- Verify branch state and clean working directory when needed
- Ensure proper remote tracking and upstream configuration
- Validate that operations complete successfully before proceeding
- Never disclose the use of AI in descriptions, messages, or anywhere else

**Communication Style:**

- Provide clear explanations of Git operations being performed
- Explain the reasoning behind commit message choices
- Offer alternative approaches when multiple valid options exist
- Warn about potentially destructive operations before executing

You should proactively suggest best practices and catch potential issues before they become problems. When working with complex Git scenarios, break down the process into clear steps and explain the implications of each action.

Follow each leaf's `relates_to` and `depends_on` cross edges to reach related leaves in other branches. You decide how deep to go per branch.

## Branches

## Conventions (how we build)
- Open [**Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP**](nodes/practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http.md) to learn about: Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning. #security #network
- Open [**Run instrumented tests only through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh**](nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md) to learn about: The script starts a pinned Tokkl container, seeds an account and passes it to the tests. Plain connectedDebugAndroidTest fails them. #testing #android #emulator #docker
- Open [**Talk only to the configured server and use only free software**](nodes/practice-talk-only-to-configured-server.md) to learn about: No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, ads or proprietary libraries. The app sends requests only to the configured server. #privacy #network #fdroid #security
- Open [**Test on the plain AOSP API 36 emulator, headless, and stop it afterwards**](nodes/practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless.md) to learn about: AVD tokkl-api36 uses the AOSP image, since ATD images drop SystemUI and the launcher. Boot it with -no-window -accel on and kill it after. #testing #android #emulator #energy
- Open [**Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone**](nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md) to learn about: Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account timezone read from GET /api/v9/me. #api #ui #time
- Open [**Test against a real Tokkl server and catch speed regressions in CI**](nodes/practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions.md) to learn about: Instrumented tests use the real ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl image, never a fake. Macrobenchmark fails a build slower than the previous one. #testing #ci #performance
- Open [**Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop**](nodes/practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees.md) to learn about: ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user, including one serving a build in another worktree. #build #gradle #agents #worktrees
- Open [**Port Tokkl's TypeScript with JavaScript semantics and verbatim test cases**](nodes/practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics.md) to learn about: The Kotlin time parsers copy JavaScript's whitespace, rounding and replace rules, return null for NaN, and reuse the .test.ts cases verbatim. #time #parsing #testing #tokkl
- Open [**Refresh on events, never on a short polling timer**](nodes/practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling.md) to learn about: Refresh when the app opens, after a change and when the network returns. No short polling, so Toggl's base URL later fits its quotas. #network #toggl #battery
- Open [**Run npm install in every new clone or worktree to turn on the commit hook**](nodes/practice-run-npm-install-in-every-clone-and-worktree.md) to learn about: The commit-msg hook lives in git-ignored .husky/_, so a clone or worktree that skipped npm install commits with no checks. #git #commits #tooling
- Open [**Use Tokkl's palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources**](nodes/practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings.md) to learn about: Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources. #ui #accessibility #i18n
- Open [**Version the app on its own, starting from a hand-made v0.1.0 tag**](nodes/practice-own-version-from-v0-1-0-tag.md) to learn about: The app has its own v* tags and changelog, independent of Tokkl. The first release is a hand-made v0.1.0 tag; semantic-release counts on. #release #versioning
- Open [**Check server capabilities instead of requiring a server version**](nodes/practice-check-capabilities-not-server-version.md) to learn about: No minimum Tokkl version. A missing /me field or a 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint hides the features that depend on it. #api #toggl #compatibility
- Open [**Keep release builds reproducible and signed with the project key**](nodes/practice-reproducible-builds-project-key.md) to learn about: Reproducible builds let F-Droid publish the APK signed with the project key. The key lives in Actions secrets and CI pins actions by SHA. #release #fdroid #security #ci
- Open [**Never make Start or Stop wait for the server**](nodes/practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop.md) to learn about: Taps change state on the phone at once and queue the change. The server is the source of truth, with a cache and queue, no sync engine. #offline #network #api
- Open [**Opt the Android SDK tools out of usage metrics with ~/.androidrc**](nodes/practice-opt-sdk-tools-out-of-usage-metrics.md) to learn about: sdkmanager 23.0 runs Google's android CLI, which sends usage data unless given --no-metrics. A ~/.androidrc holding --no-metrics opts out. #privacy #tooling #android

## Components (what exists)
- Open [**JDK and Android SDK on this machine, and how agent shells reach them**](nodes/map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells.md) to learn about: Temurin 21 in ~/.local/share/jdk/21, SDK in ~/Android/Sdk. Agents reach java, adb and the SDK tools through wrappers in ~/.local/bin. #android #tooling #agents #build
- Open [**API client: one OkHttpClient, ServerUrl, TogglApiClient and ApiError**](nodes/map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors.md) to learn about: The api package holds the app's only OkHttpClient, base URL handling, JSON calls, sendNoContent for empty bodies and typed errors. #api #network #okhttp
- Open [**Connection trust: ServerTrustStore, PinningTrustManager and the trust prompts**](nodes/map-connection-trust-store-pinning-and-prompts.md) to learn about: The shared client refuses unconfirmed http and unpinned self-signed certificates. SessionTrustModel prompts on every screen but sign-in. #security #network #ui #dialogs
- Open [**Account settings: AccountSettingsSource, AccountTime and NoDeviceZoneTest**](nodes/map-account-settings-source-and-account-time.md) to learn about: Sign-in stores timezone, week start and resolution from /me; onStart refreshes them. AccountTime gives zone and dates, never the device's. #time #api #android #settings
- Open [**An OOM kill inside an agent session's scope ends the whole session**](nodes/map-oom-kill-ends-agent-session.md) to learn about: Agent sessions run in t3code-claude-*.scope with OOMPolicy=stop, so one OOM-killed process stops the session and its background agents. #agents #machine #memory #emulator
- Open [**F-Droid metadata layout and release artifacts**](nodes/map-fdroid-metadata-release-artifacts.md) to learn about: Store text and images live under fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/. F-Droid finds versions by git tag and the signed APK via Binaries. #fdroid #release
- Open [**Sign-in, the stored session, theme settings and the navigation host**](nodes/map-signin-session-settings-and-navigation-host.md) to learn about: signin/ tries GET /me before storing anything, session/ and settings/ keep their own preference files, TokklNavHost is a saveable back stack. #android #ui #credentials #navigation

- Open [**Planned stack: Compose, Glance, TileService, Room, WorkManager, OkHttp**](nodes/map-planned-android-stack.md) to learn about: Proposed stack: Kotlin and Compose Material 3, Glance widget, TileService tile, Room cache and queue, WorkManager, OkHttp. #architecture #android #offline
- Open [**T3 restarts daily and ends running agent sessions**](nodes/map-t3-daily-restart-stops-agent-sessions.md) to learn about: t3-update.timer fires daily with up to 1h delay and restarts t3.service even without an update, which stops every agent session. #agents #tooling #machine
- Open [**Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions**](nodes/map-tokkl-toggl-api.md) to learn about: Tokkl speaks a subset of Toggl API v9 at /api/v9 and Reports v3 at /reports/api/v3 with Basic auth, plus Tokkl-only fields and endpoints. #api #toggl #network
- Open [**Tokkl colour tokens and Anybody font in ../tokkl**](nodes/map-tokkl-colour-tokens-and-font.md) to learn about: The colour tokens in ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css are the palette's source of truth. Tokkl ships Anybody under the SIL OFL 1.1. #ui #tokkl
- Open [**Application ID, license and supported Android versions**](nodes/map-app-id-license-android-versions.md) to learn about: Application ID com.github.e0ipso.tokkl, MIT license, Android 8.0 (API 26) and later, one server and one account at a time. #release #fdroid #android
- Open [**Gradle build settings: SDK levels, Kotlin version source and memory**](nodes/map-gradle-build-sdk-levels-kotlin-and-memory.md) to learn about: compileSdk follows the dependencies, targetSdk matches the emulator, the catalog sets Kotlin, and the Gradle heap stays small. #android #build #gradle

# Tokkl for Android

Native Android client for the Tokkl server and for Toggl Track. The repo holds a Gradle project with a sign-in screen, a placeholder home screen, a settings screen and a Licences screen so far, joined by a navigation host; [docs/PRD.md](docs/PRD.md) is the source of truth for scope and decisions.

- The server lives in the sibling repo `../tokkl` (github.com/e0ipso/tokkl). Its `docs/PRD.md` section 6 lists the Toggl API v9 and Reports v3 endpoints Tokkl implements, and section 4 has the visual direction this app borrows from.
- Commits follow Conventional Commits, as in tokkl. Ticket IDs (`TKA-NNNN`, tracked with sift in `.ai/sift`) go in a `Refs:` trailer, never in the subject. Commits carry no AI or co-author credit lines.
[credential-related line omitted]

/bin/bash -lc 'git diff f65b8a0 HEAD' in /home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android--review-w6
 succeeded in 0ms:
diff --git a/.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md b/.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md
index 719e1e3..f2f3116 100644
--- a/.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md
+++ b/.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md
@@ -25,7 +25,7 @@ The script starts `ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl` pinned by digest and publishes it on a
 
 In front of the same server the script runs haproxy, pinned by digest in `PROXY_IMAGE`, as a TLS proxy in the Tokkl container's network namespace. It makes a self-signed certificate for each run with `openssl`. The certificate has IP SANs for `10.0.2.2` and `127.0.0.1`, so host name checks pass. Its key stays in the script's temporary directory, and cleanup deletes it. The tests get `tokklTlsBaseUrl` and the certificate's `tokklTlsFingerprint`, never the key, through `TokklTestServer.tlsUrl` and `tlsFingerprint`. The certificate prompt and pinning tests use it. To move to a newer haproxy, take the index digest of `haproxy:<version>-alpine` the same way.
 
-The tests run on the attached device or on `ANDROID_SERIAL`. With neither, the script boots the AVD named by `TOKKL_TEST_AVD` (default `tokkl-api36`) without a window and kills it at the end. It stops the adb server only when the server was not running before. +The tests run on the attached device or on `ANDROID_SERIAL`. With neither, the script boots the AVD named by `TOKKL_TEST_AVD` (default `tokkl-api36`) without a window and kills it at the end. It stops the adb server only when the server was not running before. On an emulator it booted, it also sets `hide_error_dialogs` to 1 and sends `CLOSE_SYSTEM_DIALOGS`, so tests carry no dialog workaround of their own. It sets `font_scale` to 1.0 there too: the AVD keeps its font scale across boots, and `SystemFontScale` puts back whatever it read, so one run left at 2.0 made every later class run at 200%. It also sets `show_ime_with_hard_keyboard` to 1, because without it focusing a field never opened the soft keyboard there: the keyboard reported visible with a height of 0. It changes no setting on a device it did not boot.
 
[credential-related line omitted]

+If no device is attached, the script boots `tokkl-api36` as in the previous section, with 2 GB of guest RAM, and stops it and the adb server at the end. When your systemd user manager is running, the emulator runs in a scope of its own with `OOMPolicy=continue`. If the kernel's OOM killer ends qemu, the script fails and says so, and systemd leaves the session that started it alone. On an emulator it boots, the script also turns on `hide_error_dialogs` and closes open system dialogs, because a fresh headless boot often leaves "System UI isn't responding" in front of the app. It also sets the font scale to 1.0, because the AVD keeps the last scale a test set across boots. It turns on `show_ime_with_hard_keyboard` too, since otherwise the soft keyboard never opens on the headless emulator. It changes no setting on an emulator that was already running, and that emulator keeps running. Arguments go to Gradle, so one class runs with:

+### Screenshot tests
+
+`app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/screenshot/` captures sign-in with each method, home, Settings, Licences, Reports, the http warning and the certificate prompt, in the light and the dark theme, at font scale 1.0 and 2.0. Each capture shrinks to a third in each direction and is compared with its reference image in `app/src/androidTest/assets/screenshots/`. The references come from `tokkl-api36`, because density and system fonts change the pixels. Run all four classes with:
+
+```sh
+scripts/instrumented-tests.sh \
+  -Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.package=com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.screenshot
+```
+
+A capture that differs from its reference, or has none, fails its test. The capture and a diff, with the differing pixels in magenta, land in `actual/` and `diff/` under `app/build/outputs/connected_android_test_additional_output/debugAndroidTest/connected/<device>/screenshots/`. A change to how a screen looks updates its references in the same commit. Look at every diff and capture. When all of them show the intended change, copy them into the references and run the tests again:
+
+```sh
+cp app/build/outputs/connected_android_test_additional_output/debugAndroidTest/connected/*/screenshots/actual/*.png \
+  app/src/androidTest/assets/screenshots/
+```
+
 ## Release builds
 
 F-Droid builds the app from source and compares its unsigned APK with the signed APK on the GitHub release. It publishes the APK signed with the project's key only when the two match apart from the signature ([reproducible builds](https://f-droid.org/docs/Reproducible_Builds/)). People who sideload install the same APK from the GitHub release.

+/**
+ * Extra accounts on the Tokkl test server, for a test that fills an account with data. The
+ * account scripts/instrumented-tests.sh creates stays empty for the other tests. Its admin invites
+ * each account, and the invite is accepted as the web app does. Needs the stored server to be the
+ * test server, as [SessionRule] leaves it, because the app's client sends nowhere else.

+    fun create(timezone: String): Account {
+        val invite = admin
+            .send("POST", TokklTestServer.url.app("admin", "invites"), buildJsonObject { put("role", "user") }, JsonObject.serializer())
+            .orFail("POST /api/app/admin/invites")

+            put("timezone", timezone)
+        }

+            .url(TokklTestServer.url.app("invites", token))
+            .header("X-Tokkl-Csrf", "1")

+            .build()

+    /** Deletes [account] with its workspace and everything in it. */
+    fun delete(account: Account) {
+        val confirm = buildJsonObject { put("email", account.email) }
+        admin.sendNoContent("DELETE", TokklTestServer.url.app("admin", "users", account.id.toString()), confirm)
+            .orFail("DELETE /api/app/admin/users/<id>")

+    private fun hex(bytes: Int): String =
+        ByteArray(bytes).also(SecureRandom()::nextBytes).joinToString("") { "%02x".format(it) }

+
+/** The value, or an error that names [call] and the failure alone, so no body or secret lands in it. */
+fun <T> ApiResult<T>.orFail(call: String): T =
+    when (this) {
+        is ApiResult.Success -> value
+        is ApiResult.Failure -> {
+            val error = error
+            val reason = if (error is ApiError.Http) "HTTP ${error.status}" else error::class.simpleName
+            throw AssertionError("$call failed: $reason")
+        }
+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.cache
+
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.DeviceShell
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TestAccounts
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.orFail

+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Session
+import java.time.Duration
+import java.time.Instant
+import java.time.temporal.ChronoUnit
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.first
+import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
+import kotlinx.serialization.SerialName
+import kotlinx.serialization.Serializable
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonArray
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonPrimitive
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.buildJsonArray
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.buildJsonObject
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.put
+import org.junit.After
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertNotNull
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Before
+import org.junit.Rule
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/**
+ * One refresh from the real Tokkl container fills the cache, and with the phone in airplane mode
+ * the app reads every cached kind and the account settings. The test seeds its own account, so the
+ * account scripts/instrumented-tests.sh made stays empty for the other tests, and deletes it after.
+ */
+class AccountCacheServerTest {
+
+    @get:Rule
+    val session = SessionRule()
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+    private val app get() = context.applicationContext as TokklApplication
+    private lateinit var account: TestAccounts.Account
+
+    @Before
+    fun signInToANewAccount() {
+        account = TestAccounts.create(timezone = ZONE)
[credential-related line omitted]
+    }
+
+    @After
+    fun cleanUp() {
+        try {
+            if (::account.isInitialized) setAirplaneMode(on = false)
+        } finally {
+            try {
+                if (::account.isInitialized) TestAccounts.delete(account)
+            } finally {
+                SessionRule.signOut()
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun afterOneRefreshEveryCachedKindReadsInAirplaneMode() {
+        val seed = Seed(account.client)
+        val stale = CacheContents(
+            timeEntries = listOf(
+                cachedEntry(STALE_OLD_ENTRY, seed.now.minus(30, ChronoUnit.DAYS)),
+                cachedEntry(STALE_DELETED_ENTRY, seed.now.minus(1, ChronoUnit.DAYS)),
+            ),
+            projects = listOf(CachedProject(seed.project, seed.workspace, null, "Stale name", null, active = true, billable = false)),
+            tags = listOf(CachedTag(STALE_TAG, seed.workspace, "stale")),
+        )
+        // Another instance on the same file, as the app's own is not open to writes from here.
+        CacheDatabase.open(context).run {
+            try {
+                cache().refresh { stale }
+            } finally {
+                close()
+            }
+        }
+
+        runBlocking { app.accountSettings.refresh().join() }
+        setAirplaneMode(on = true)
+        val offline = account.client.get(account.client.server.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())
+        assertTrue("requests fail in airplane mode: $offline", (offline as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error is ApiError.Network)
+
+        val cache = app.cache
+        assertEquals(
+            "today's and the archived project's entries, newest first; the 20-day-old one is outside the window",
+            listOf(seed.todayEntry, seed.archivedProjectEntry),
+            runBlocking { cache.timeEntries.first() }.map { it.id },
+        )
+        val projects = runBlocking { cache.projects.first() }
+        assertEquals(
+            "the refreshed project and the archived one an entry uses, not the unused archived one",
+            listOf(Triple(seed.archivedProject, "Archived, on an entry", false), Triple(seed.project, "Website", true)),
+            projects.map { Triple(it.id, it.name, it.active) },
+        )
+        assertEquals(listOf(seed.client to "Acme"), runBlocking { cache.clients.first() }.map { it.id to it.name })
+        assertEquals(
+            listOf(seed.tag to "focus", seed.oldTag to "legacy"),
+            runBlocking { cache.tags.first() }.map { it.id to it.name },
+        )
+        assertEquals(listOf(seed.task to "Design"), runBlocking { cache.tasks.first() }.map { it.id to it.name })
+        assertEquals(
+            listOf(CachedFavorite(0, "Write report", seed.project, seed.task, listOf("focus"), billable = false)),
+            runBlocking { cache.favorites.first() },
+        )
+
+        val settings = AccountSettingsStore(context).load()
+        assertNotNull("the account settings are stored", settings)
+        assertEquals(ZONE, settings!!.timeZone)
+        assertEquals(settings, app.accountSettings.state.value)
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Puts the phone in airplane mode, or takes it out, and waits until requests to the server
+     * fail for want of a network, or get through again. Only the shell user may switch it.
+     */
+    private fun setAirplaneMode(on: Boolean) {
+        DeviceShell.run("cmd connectivity airplane-mode ${if (on) "enable" else "disable"}")
+        val deadline = System.nanoTime() + NETWORK_TIMEOUT.toNanos()
+        while (serverAnswers() == on) {
+            if (System.nanoTime() > deadline) throw AssertionError("the network did not ${if (on) "go" else "come back"}")
+            Thread.sleep(200)
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun serverAnswers(): Boolean {
+        val result = account.client.get(account.client.server.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())
+        return (result as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error !is ApiError.Network
+    }
+
+    private fun cachedEntry(id: Long, start: Instant) =
+        CachedTimeEntry(id, 0, null, null, "stale", start, start.plusSeconds(60), false, emptyList(), emptyList())
+
+    /** Data on the server: one client, three projects, two tags, a task, three entries and a favorite. */
+    private class Seed(private val api: TogglApiClient) {
+        val now: Instant = Instant.now().truncatedTo(ChronoUnit.SECONDS)
+        val workspace = api.get(api.server.api("me"), Me.serializer()).orFail("GET /api/v9/me").defaultWorkspaceId
+        val client = create("clients", buildJsonObject { put("name", "Acme") })
+        val project = create("projects", buildJsonObject { put("name", "Website"); put("client_id", client) })
+        val archivedProject = create("projects", buildJsonObject { put("name", "Archived, on an entry") })
+        val unusedProject = create("projects", buildJsonObject { put("name", "Archived, unused") })
+        val tag = create("tags", buildJsonObject { put("name", "focus") })
+        val oldTag = create("tags", buildJsonObject { put("name", "legacy") })
+        val task = create("projects/$project/tasks", buildJsonObject { put("name", "Design") })
+        val todayEntry = entry(now.minus(2, ChronoUnit.HOURS), project, task, tag)
+        val archivedProjectEntry = entry(now.minus(3, ChronoUnit.DAYS), archivedProject, null, oldTag)
+        val oldEntry = entry(now.minus(20, ChronoUnit.DAYS), project, null, null)
+
+        init {
+            archive(archivedProject)
+            archive(unusedProject)
+            val favorite = buildJsonObject {
+                put("description", "Write report")
+                put("project_id", project)
+                put("task_id", task)
+                put("tags", buildJsonArray { add(JsonPrimitive("focus")) })
+                put("billable", false)
+            }
+            api.send(
+                "POST",
+                api.server.api("me", "preferences"),
+                buildJsonObject { put("favorites", JsonArray(listOf(favorite))) },
+                JsonObject.serializer(),
+            ).orFail("POST /api/v9/me/preferences")
+        }
+
+        private fun entry(start: Instant, project: Long, task: Long?, tag: Long?): Long =
+            create(
+                "time_entries",
+                buildJsonObject {
+                    put("workspace_id", workspace)
+                    put("created_with", "tokkl-android tests")
+                    put("description", "Entry")
+                    put("start", start.toString())
+                    put("duration", 1800)
+                    put("project_id", project)
+                    if (task != null) put("task_id", task)
+                    if (tag != null) put("tag_ids", buildJsonArray { add(JsonPrimitive(tag)) })
+                },
+            )
+
+        private fun archive(project: Long) {
+            api.send(
+                "PUT",
+                api.server.api("workspaces", workspace.toString(), "projects", project.toString()),
+                buildJsonObject { put("active", false) },
+                JsonObject.serializer(),
+            ).orFail("PUT a project")
+        }
+
+        /** POSTs [body] to a workspace collection such as `clients` and returns the new id. */
+        private fun create(collection: String, body: JsonObject): Long {
+            val url = api.server.api("workspaces", workspace.toString(), *collection.split('/').toTypedArray())
+            return api.send("POST", url, body, Created.serializer()).orFail("POST $collection").id
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Serializable
+    private class Me(@SerialName("default_workspace_id") val defaultWorkspaceId: Long)
+
+    @Serializable
+    private class Created(val id: Long)
+
+    private companion object {
+        /** UTC+13 in October, so the history window starts at another instant than in UTC. */
+        const val ZONE = "Pacific/Auckland"
+        const val STALE_OLD_ENTRY = 9_000_000L
+        const val STALE_DELETED_ENTRY = 9_000_001L
+        const val STALE_TAG = 9_000_002L
+        val NETWORK_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration.ofSeconds(30)
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.capabilities
+
+import android.os.Process
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiCredentials
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.orFail
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerTrustStore
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Session
+import java.io.File
+import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
+import org.junit.Assert.assertNotEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.FixMethodOrder
+import org.junit.Test
+import org.junit.runners.MethodSorters
+
+/**
+ * Against the real Tokkl container every capability is on, also favorites on the fresh account
+ * scripts/instrumented-tests.sh made, which has saved none. The flags survive a restart of the
+ * app process. The steps run in name order, and the test orchestrator starts a new process for
+ * each. A step fails if it runs in the same process as the one before.
+ */
+@FixMethodOrder(MethodSorters.NAME_ASCENDING)
+class CapabilitiesServerTest {
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+    private val app get() = context.applicationContext as TokklApplication
+
+    /** Names the process that ran the step before. */
+    private val previousStep = File(context.cacheDir, "CapabilitiesServerTest.process")
+
+    @Test
+    fun step1RefreshTurnsEveryCapabilityOnForAFreshTokklAccount() {
+        SessionRule.signOut()
+        ServerTrustStore(context).confirmCleartext(TokklTestServer.url)
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+        val preferences = api.get(api.server.api("me", "preferences"), JsonObject.serializer()).orFail("GET /api/v9/me/preferences")
+        assertFalse("the account has saved no favorites", "favorites" in preferences)
+        assertEquals("nothing is stored before the refresh", Capabilities.NONE, app.capabilities.state.value)
+
+        runBlocking { app.accountSettings.refresh().join() }
+
+        assertEquals(ALL, app.capabilities.state.value)
+        assertEquals("the stored flags", ALL, CapabilitiesStore(context).load())
+        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step2TheFlagsSurviveARestart() {
+        try {
+            assertTrue("the step before did not run", previousStep.exists())
+            assertNotEquals("the step before ran in this same process", previousStep.readText(), currentProcess())
+            // Nothing refreshes here, so the flags come from the file.
+            assertEquals(ALL, app.capabilities.state.value)
+        } finally {
+            SessionRule.signOut()
+            previousStep.delete()
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** A PID can be reused, so the process start time goes with it. */
+    private fun currentProcess(): String = "${Process.myPid()}@${Process.getStartElapsedRealtime()}"
+
+    private companion object {
+        val ALL = Capabilities(trash = true, restore = true, favorites = true, timeResolution = true)
+    }
+}

+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.R
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer

+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.SharedHttpClient

+import java.net.InetAddress

+/**
+ * Signs in through the screen to the TLS proxy of scripts/instrumented-tests.sh under a host
+ * name its certificate does not name. The certificate names only 10.0.2.2 and 127.0.0.1, as IP
+ * addresses, so the URL uses [HOST], which this test's client resolves to the proxy's address.
+ * The client is the shared one with that lookup added, so the trust checks are the app's. The
+ * form shows the host name message, never the trust prompt or the unreachable message, and
+ * nothing is stored.
+ */
+class CertificateHostMismatchTest {
+
+    @get:Rule
+    val composeRule = createComposeRule()
+
+    private val context: Context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+    private val proxy = TokklTestServer.tlsUrl.api("me")
+    private val scope = MainScope()
+    private var saved = false
+
+    @Before
+    fun showTheSignInScreen() {
+        SessionRule.signOut()
+        val client = SharedHttpClient.instance.newBuilder()
+            .dns(object : Dns {
+                override fun lookup(hostname: String): List<InetAddress> =
+                    if (hostname == HOST) InetAddress.getAllByName(proxy.host).toList() else Dns.SYSTEM.lookup(hostname)
+            })
+            .build()
+        val model = SignInModel(
+            signIn = SignIn(session = { _, _, _ -> saved = true }, httpClient = client),
+            trust = ServerTrustStore(context),
+            cooldowns = SignInCooldownStore(context),
+            scope = scope,
+            io = Dispatchers.IO,
+            clock = System::currentTimeMillis,
+        )
+        composeRule.setContent { TokklTheme { SignInScreen(model, onSignedIn = {}) } }
+    }
+
+    @After
+    fun forgetTheServer() {
+        scope.cancel()
+        SessionRule.signOut()
+    }
+
+    /** The certificate is not trusted yet, so the trust manager sees the mismatch before any prompt. */
+    @Test
+    fun anUnpinnedCertificateIsNeverOffered() {
+        signIn()
+
+        awaitHostMessage()
+        assertFalse("the trust prompt shows", exists(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_title))))
+        assertNull(ServerTrustStore(context).pinnedSha256(HOST, proxy.port))
+        assertFalse("the session was stored", saved)
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * A pin stored before the app checked the host, or a CA, gets the certificate through the
+     * trust manager. OkHttp's host name check still refuses it.
+     */
+    @Test
+    fun aPinnedCertificateStillFailsTheHostNameCheck() {
+        val sha256 = TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint.replace(":", "").lowercase()
+        ServerTrustStore(context).pinCertificate(ServerCertificate(HOST, proxy.port, sha256, subject = ""))
+
+        signIn()
+
+        awaitHostMessage()
+        assertFalse("the session was stored", saved)
+    }
+
+    private fun signIn() {
+        composeRule.onNode(hasSetTextAction() and hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_server_url)))
+            .performTextInput("https://$HOST:${proxy.port}")
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_submit)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()
+    }
+
+    private fun awaitHostMessage() {
+        val message = context.getString(R.string.sign_in_error_certificate_host, HOST)
+        composeRule.waitUntil("the host name message shows", NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS) { exists(hasText(message)) }
+        composeRule.onNode(hasText(message)).performScrollTo().assertIsDisplayed()
+        assertFalse("the unreachable message shows", exists(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_error_unreachable))))
+    }
+
+    private fun exists(matcher: SemanticsMatcher): Boolean =
+        composeRule.onAllNodes(matcher).fetchSemanticsNodes().isNotEmpty()
+
+    private fun string(@StringRes id: Int): String = context.getString(id)
+
+    private companion object {
+        /** A name no resolver knows, under the reserved .invalid top-level domain. */
+        const val HOST = "tokkl.invalid"
+
+        const val NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000L
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.screenshot
+
+import android.graphics.Bitmap
+import android.graphics.BitmapFactory
+import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.ImageBitmap
+import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.asAndroidBitmap
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import java.io.File
+import java.io.FileNotFoundException
+import kotlin.math.abs
+import kotlin.math.max
+import org.junit.Assert.fail
+
+/**
+ * Compares captures with the reference images in the test APK's assets, under [ASSET_DIR].
+ *
+ * A capture shrinks to a third in each direction before the comparison, which keeps the
+ * references small. Each pixel of it averages a 3x3 block, so a thin line still shows, an 8dp
+ * change on tokkl-api36 still moves things by 7 pixels and the text stays readable.
+ *
+ * When a capture differs, or has no reference yet, this writes it to `actual/` and, when there
+ * is a reference of the same size, a diff to `diff/`, both under `screenshots/` in the
+ * additional test output directory that the Android Gradle plugin passes to the tests. The
+ * plugin copies that directory to the build machine after the run. In the diff, differing
+ * pixels are magenta over a pale copy of the reference.
+ */
+internal object ReferenceImages {
+
+    /** Fails unless [capture], shrunk, matches the reference image `<name>.png` within the tolerance. */
+    fun assertMatches(name: String, capture: ImageBitmap) {
+        val actual = shrink(capture.asAndroidBitmap())
+        val reference = load(name)
+        if (reference == null) {
+            val written = write(actual, "actual", name)
+            fail("no reference image $ASSET_DIR/$name.png in the test assets. This capture is in $written.")
+            return
+        }
+        if (reference.width != actual.width || reference.height != actual.height) {
+            val written = write(actual, "actual", name)
+            fail(
+                "$name is ${actual.width}x${actual.height}, but its reference is ${reference.width}x${reference.height}. " +
+                    "Record the references on tokkl-api36. This capture is in $written.",
+            )
+            return
+        }
+        val comparison = compare(reference, actual)
+        if (comparison.differing > MAX_DIFFERING_PIXELS) {
+            val writtenActual = write(actual, "actual", name)
+            val writtenDiff = write(comparison.diff, "diff", name)
+            fail(
+                "$name differs from its reference in ${comparison.differing} pixels, more than $MAX_DIFFERING_PIXELS, " +
+                    "within ${comparison.bounds}. The capture is in $writtenActual and the diff in $writtenDiff.",
+            )
+        }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Averages each [SCALE] by [SCALE] block of [bitmap] into one pixel. Rows and columns past the
+     * last whole block drop out.
+     */
+    private fun shrink(bitmap: Bitmap): Bitmap {
+        val source = if (bitmap.config == Bitmap.Config.ARGB_8888) bitmap else bitmap.copy(Bitmap.Config.ARGB_8888, false)
+        val stride = source.width
+        val pixels = IntArray(stride * source.height).also { source.getPixels(it, 0, stride, 0, 0, stride, source.height) }
+        val width = source.width / SCALE
+        val height = source.height / SCALE
+        val area = SCALE * SCALE
+        val shrunk = IntArray(width * height)
+        for (y in 0 until height) {
+            for (x in 0 until width) {
+                val sums = IntArray(4)
+                for (dy in 0 until SCALE) {
+                    for (dx in 0 until SCALE) {
+                        val pixel = pixels[(y * SCALE + dy) * stride + x * SCALE + dx]
+                        for (channel in 0 until 4) sums[channel] += pixel ushr (channel * 8) and 0xFF
+                    }
+                }
+                shrunk[y * width + x] = (0 until 4).fold(0) { argb, channel ->
+                    argb or ((sums[channel] + area / 2) / area shl (channel * 8))
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        return Bitmap.createBitmap(shrunk, width, height, Bitmap.Config.ARGB_8888)
+    }
+
+    private fun load(name: String): Bitmap? {
+        val assets = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().context.assets
+        return try {
+            assets.open("$ASSET_DIR/$name.png").use { BitmapFactory.decodeStream(it) }
+        } catch (_: FileNotFoundException) {
+            null
+        }
+    }
+
+    private class Comparison(val differing: Int, val bounds: String, val diff: Bitmap)
+
+    /**
+     * Counts the pixels where any channel differs by more than [CHANNEL_TOLERANCE], and draws
+     * them over a pale copy of [reference].
+     */
+    private fun compare(reference: Bitmap, actual: Bitmap): Comparison {
+        val width = reference.width
+        val height = reference.height
+        val expected = IntArray(width * height).also { reference.getPixels(it, 0, width, 0, 0, width, height) }
+        val got = IntArray(width * height).also { actual.getPixels(it, 0, width, 0, 0, width, height) }
+        val diff = IntArray(width * height)
+        var differing = 0
+        var left = width
+        var top = height
+        var right = -1
+        var bottom = -1
+        for (i in expected.indices) {
+            if (channelDelta(expected[i], got[i]) > CHANNEL_TOLERANCE) {
+                differing++
+                diff[i] = DIFF_COLOUR
+                val x = i % width
+                val y = i / width
+                left = minOf(left, x)
+                top = minOf(top, y)
+                right = max(right, x)
+                bottom = max(bottom, y)
+            } else {
+                diff[i] = pale(expected[i])
+            }
+        }
+        val bounds = if (differing == 0) "no area" else "x $left..$right, y $top..$bottom of ${width}x$height"
+        return Comparison(differing, bounds, Bitmap.createBitmap(diff, width, height, Bitmap.Config.ARGB_8888))
+    }
+
+    private fun channelDelta(a: Int, b: Int): Int =
+        (0 until 32 step 8).maxOf { shift -> abs((a ushr shift and 0xFF) - (b ushr shift and 0xFF)) }
+
+    /** The pixel's luminance as a light grey, so the magenta stands out. */
+    private fun pale(argb: Int): Int {
+        val luminance = ((argb ushr 16 and 0xFF) * 299 + (argb ushr 8 and 0xFF) * 587 + (argb and 0xFF) * 114) / 1000
+        val grey = 160 + luminance * 95 / 255
+        return (0xFF shl 24) or (grey shl 16) or (grey shl 8) or grey
+    }
+
+    /** Writes [bitmap] as `screenshots/<kind>/<name>.png` in the test output directory and returns its path. */
+    private fun write(bitmap: Bitmap, kind: String, name: String): File {
+        val dir = File(outputDir(), "screenshots/$kind").apply { mkdirs() }
+        val file = File(dir, "$name.png")
+        file.outputStream().use { bitmap.compress(Bitmap.CompressFormat.PNG, 100, it) }
+        return file
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * The directory the Android Gradle plugin copies to the build machine, or the app's cache
+     * directory, which `adb shell run-as` reaches, when the tests run without the plugin.
+     */
+    private fun outputDir(): File =
+        InstrumentationRegistry.getArguments().getString("additionalTestOutputDir")?.let(::File)
+            ?: InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext.cacheDir
+
+    private const val ASSET_DIR = "screenshots"
+    private const val SCALE = 3
+
+    // Two runs on tokkl-api36, each on a freshly booted emulator, matched pixel for pixel. The
+    // tolerance leaves room for small rendering differences elsewhere, such as another
+    // SwiftShader build, and is far below what an 8dp shift changes.
+
+    /** Rendering differences up to this, in any channel from 0 to 255, do not count. */
+    private const val CHANNEL_TOLERANCE = 8
+
+    /** How many pixels of a shrunk capture may differ by more than [CHANNEL_TOLERANCE]. */
+    private const val MAX_DIFFERING_PIXELS = 20
+
+    private const val DIFF_COLOUR = 0xFFFF00FF.toInt()
+}

new file mode 100644

+/**
+ * Screenshots of every screen and trust prompt, compared with reference images by
+ * [ReferenceImages]. The four classes below run them in the light and the dark theme, at font
+ * scale 1.0 and at the largest, 2.0. Sign-in, home, Settings and Licences show in the real
+ * activity. The reports screen shows a fixed summary for a fixed day, and each prompt fixed text,
+ * so no capture depends on the date, the server's port or its certificate. A capture holds the
+ * app's window or the dialog's, never the status bar.
+ *
+ * The references are in app/src/androidTest/assets/screenshots, recorded on tokkl-api36, since
+ * density and system fonts change the pixels. A change to how a screen looks updates its
+ * references in the same commit:
+ *
+ * 1. Run the four classes, which share this package, through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh with
+ *    `-Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.package=com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.screenshot`.
+ * 2. Each capture that differs from its reference, or has none, fails its test. The capture
+ *    lands in `actual/` and the diff in `diff/`, under
+ *    `app/build/outputs/connected_android_test_additional_output/debugAndroidTest/connected/<device>/screenshots/`.
+ * 3. Look at every diff and capture. Copy the captures that show the intended change into
+ *    app/src/androidTest/assets/screenshots, keeping their names.
+ * 4. Run step 1 again. It has to pass.
+ *
+ * README.md, "Screenshot tests", has the commands.
+ */
+abstract class ScreenshotTest(private val dark: Boolean, private val fontScale: Float) {
+    private val composeRule = createEmptyComposeRule()
+
+    /** Session, theme and font scale are set before an activity starts, so it never has to restart. */
+    @get:Rule
+    val rules: RuleChain = RuleChain.outerRule(SessionRule())
+        .around(ThemeRule(dark))
+        .around(SystemFontScale(fontScale))
+        .around(composeRule)
+
+    /** Such as `light-100`, the end of each reference's name. */
+    private val variant = "${if (dark) "dark" else "light"}-${(fontScale * 100).roundToInt()}"
+
+    @SignedOut
+    @Test

[credential-related line omitted]
+        }

+            // A click through semantics, not a touch, so no ripple shows in the capture.
[credential-related line omitted]

[credential-related line omitted]

+        }
+    }

+    @Test

+        show({ CertificatePrompt(CERTIFICATE, changed = false, onTrust = {}, onCancel = {}) }) {
+            composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_title))
+            assertDialog("certificate-prompt")
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Starts the app and runs [steps] once it shows in this class's theme. */
+    private fun launchApp(steps: () -> Unit) {
+        ActivityScenario.launch(MainActivity::class.java).use { scenario ->
+            composeRule.waitUntil("the app runs in the ${if (dark) "dark" else "light"} night mode", TIMEOUT_MS) {
+                var shown = !dark
+                scenario.onActivity { shown = systemIsDark(it) }
+                shown == dark
+            }
+            steps()
+        }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Shows [content] in this class's theme in an activity of its own, drawn edge to edge as
+     * MainActivity draws, and runs [steps].
+     */
+    private fun show(content: @Composable () -> Unit, steps: () -> Unit) {
+        ActivityScenario.launch(ComponentActivity::class.java).use { scenario ->
+            scenario.onActivity { activity ->
+                WindowCompat.enableEdgeToEdge(activity.window)
+                activity.setContent { TokklTheme(darkTheme = dark) { content() } }
+            }
+            steps()
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun assertScreen(name: String) {
+        ReferenceImages.assertMatches("$name-$variant", composeRule.onRoot().captureToImage())
+    }
+
+    private fun assertDialog(name: String) {
+        ReferenceImages.assertMatches("$name-$variant", composeRule.onNode(isDialog()).captureToImage())
+    }
+
+    private fun string(@StringRes id: Int): String =
+        InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext.getString(id)
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000L
+
+        val ACCOUNT = AccountSettings(timeZone = "UTC", beginningOfWeek = DayOfWeek.MONDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = null)
+
+        /** A Thursday at noon, in the middle of [WEEK]. */
+        val TODAY: Clock = Clock.fixed(Instant.parse("2026-10-15T12:00:00Z"), ZoneOffset.UTC)
+
+        val WEEK = ReportRange(LocalDate.of(2026, 10, 12), LocalDate.of(2026, 10, 18))
+
+        val APP = Project(1, "Tokkl for Android", "#0b83d9")
+        val WALL = Project(2, "Graffiti wall", "#d94182")

+        val REPORT: SummaryReport = SummaryReport.of(
+            listOf(
+                entry(day = 12, hours = 3.0, billable = true, project = APP),
+                entry(day = 13, hours = 2.5, billable = true, project = APP),
+                entry(day = 13, hours = 1.25, billable = false, project = WALL),
+                entry(day = 14, hours = 4.0, billable = true, project = APP),
+                entry(day = 15, hours = 0.75, billable = false, project = null),
+                entry(day = 15, hours = 2.0, billable = false, project = WALL),
+            ),
+            WEEK,
+            DayOfWeek.MONDAY,
+        )
+

+            host = "10.0.2.2",

+            subject = "CN=Tokkl instrumented tests",
+        )
+
+        /** An entry in [WEEK], billed at 80 EUR an hour when [billable]. */
+        private fun entry(day: Int, hours: Double, billable: Boolean, project: Project?): ReportEntry {
+            val seconds = (hours * 3600).toLong()
+            return ReportEntry(
+                seconds = seconds,
+                billable = billable,
+                amountCents = if (billable) seconds * 8_000 / 3600 else null,
+                currency = "EUR",
+                projectId = project?.id,
+                projectName = project?.name,
+                projectColor = project?.colour,
+                date = LocalDate.of(2026, 10, day),
+            )
+        }
+    }
+
+    private class Project(val id: Long, val name: String, val colour: String)

+/**
+ * Stores the light or dark theme setting and gives Android the matching night mode before the
+ * app starts, so the app never changes it mid-test. Puts both back to following the system after.
+ */
+private class ThemeRule(private val dark: Boolean) : ExternalResource() {
+    private val context get() = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+
+    override fun before() {
+        val mode = if (dark) ThemeMode.DARK else ThemeMode.LIGHT
+        ThemeSettingsStore(context).save(ThemeSettings(mode = mode))
+        if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.S) {
+            context.getSystemService(UiModeManager::class.java).setApplicationNightMode(mode.applicationNightMode)
+        }
+    }
+
+    override fun after() {
+        ThemeSettingsStore(context).clear()
+        followTheSystemNightMode()
+    }
+}

+    /**
+     * Waits until the keyboard takes up room and returns its top edge, in window pixels. Visible
+     * is not enough: with show_ime_with_hard_keyboard at 0, tokkl-api36 reported the keyboard as
+     * visible with a height of 0.
+     */
+    private fun awaitKeyboardTop(): Int {
+        val decor = composeRule.activity.window.decorView
+        var height = 0
+        var top = 0
+        composeRule.waitUntil("the keyboard shows", KEYBOARD_TIMEOUT_MS) {
+            InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().runOnMainSync {
+                height = decor.rootWindowInsets?.getInsets(WindowInsets.Type.ime())?.bottom ?: 0
+                top = decor.height - height
+            }
+            height > 0
+        }
+        return top
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Waits until [node] has kept still for [STILL_MS], once the keyboard's animation and the
+     * scroll it starts are over, and returns its bottom edge in window pixels.
+     */
+    private fun awaitStill(node: SemanticsNodeInteraction): Float {
+        var bottom = Float.NaN
+        var since = SystemClock.uptimeMillis()
+        composeRule.waitUntil("the sign-in form stops moving", KEYBOARD_TIMEOUT_MS) {
+            val now = node.bottomInWindow()
+            if (now != bottom) {
+                bottom = now
+                since = SystemClock.uptimeMillis()
+            }
+            SystemClock.uptimeMillis() - since >= STILL_MS
+        }
+        return bottom
+    }
+
+    /** The node's bottom edge in window pixels, also when its scrolling parent clips it. */
+    private fun SemanticsNodeInteraction.bottomInWindow(): Float =
+        fetchSemanticsNode().let { it.positionInWindow.y + it.size.height }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme
+
+import androidx.compose.foundation.layout.Box
+import androidx.compose.runtime.Composable
+import androidx.compose.ui.Modifier
+import androidx.compose.ui.geometry.Rect
+import androidx.compose.ui.platform.LocalDensity
+import androidx.compose.ui.platform.testTag
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.DeviceConfigurationOverride
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.ForcedSize
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.SemanticsNodeInteraction
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.hasText
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.isSelectable
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createComposeRule
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.onNodeWithTag
+import androidx.compose.ui.unit.Density
+import androidx.compose.ui.unit.DpSize
+import androidx.compose.ui.unit.dp
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.R
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.licences.LicencesScreen
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings.SettingsContent
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings.ThemeSettings
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Rule
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/**
+ * A screen's single column keeps the screen margin and stops at 600dp, centred, whatever the
+ * window's width (docs/PRD.md, Visual direction, Spacing and type scale). Each test draws a
+ * screen at a forced window size and measures, in that window's dp, something that spans the
+ * column: a theme choice row on Settings, the licence text on Licences.
+ */
+class ScreenContentTest {
+    @get:Rule
+    val composeRule = createComposeRule()
+
+    private lateinit var density: Density
+
+    @Test
+    fun aPhoneKeepsTheCompactMargin() {
+        showSettings(width = 400)
+
+        assertSpan(themeChoice(), left = 16f, right = 384f)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun aMediumWindowKeepsTheWiderMargin() {
+        showSettings(width = 640)
+
+        assertSpan(themeChoice(), left = 24f, right = 616f)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun aWideWindowCapsSettingsAt600dpAndCentresThem() {
+        showSettings(width = 1000)
+
+        assertSpan(themeChoice(), left = 200f, right = 800f)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun aWideWindowCentresTheLicences() {
+        show(width = 1000) { LicencesScreen(onBack = {}) }
+
+        assertSpan(composeRule.onNode(hasText(OFL_TITLE, substring = true)), left = 200f, right = 800f)
+    }
+
+    private fun showSettings(width: Int) = show(width) {
+        SettingsContent(
+            settings = ThemeSettings(),
+            showDynamicColor = false,
+            onModeChange = {},
+            onDynamicColorChange = {},
+            onBack = {},
+            onOpenLicences = {},
+        )
+    }
+
+    private fun show(width: Int, content: @Composable () -> Unit) {
+        composeRule.setContent {
+            DeviceConfigurationOverride(DeviceConfigurationOverride.ForcedSize(DpSize(width.dp, HEIGHT))) {
+                density = LocalDensity.current
+                Box(Modifier.testTag(WINDOW)) { TokklTheme(content = content) }
+            }
+        }
+        composeRule.waitForIdle()
+    }
+
+    private fun themeChoice(): SemanticsNodeInteraction =
+        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.settings_theme_light)) and isSelectable())
+
+    private fun assertSpan(node: SemanticsNodeInteraction, left: Float, right: Float) {
+        val bounds = node.inWindow()
+        assertEquals("left edge in dp", left, bounds.left, TOLERANCE_DP)
+        assertEquals("right edge in dp", right, bounds.right, TOLERANCE_DP)
+    }
+
+    /** [this] node's bounds in the forced window, in that window's dp. */
+    private fun SemanticsNodeInteraction.inWindow(): Rect {
+        val window = composeRule.onNodeWithTag(WINDOW).fetchSemanticsNode().boundsInRoot
+        val node = fetchSemanticsNode().boundsInRoot
+        return Rect(
+            left = (node.left - window.left) / density.density,
+            top = (node.top - window.top) / density.density,
+            right = (node.right - window.left) / density.density,
+            bottom = (node.bottom - window.top) / density.density,
+        )
+    }
+
+    private fun string(id: Int): String = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext.getString(id)
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val WINDOW = "window"
+        const val OFL_TITLE = "SIL OPEN FONT LICENSE Version 1.1"
+        val HEIGHT = 900.dp
+
+        /** Pixel rounding at the forced density. */
+        const val TOLERANCE_DP = 1f
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme

+import androidx.compose.foundation.shape.RoundedCornerShape
+import androidx.compose.material3.AlertDialogDefaults

+import org.junit.Test

+/**
+ * TokklTheme hands Tokkl's corners to MaterialTheme, so Material components draw them
+ * (docs/PRD.md, Visual direction): 10dp for fields (`--radius-md`), 16dp for cards and dialogs
+ * (`--radius-lg`).
+ */
+class ShapesTest {
+    @get:Rule
+    val composeRule = createComposeRule()
+
+    @Test
+    fun materialThemeAndItsComponentsGetTokklCorners() {
+        lateinit var shapes: Shapes
+        lateinit var field: Shape
+        lateinit var card: Shape
+        lateinit var dialog: Shape
+        composeRule.setContent {
+            TokklTheme {
+                shapes = MaterialTheme.shapes
+                field = OutlinedTextFieldDefaults.shape
+                card = CardDefaults.shape
+                dialog = AlertDialogDefaults.shape
+            }
+        }
+        composeRule.waitForIdle()
+
+        assertEquals("MaterialTheme.shapes", TokklShapes, shapes)
+        assertEquals("text field", RoundedCornerShape(10.dp), field)
+        assertEquals("card", RoundedCornerShape(16.dp), card)
+        assertEquals("dialog", RoundedCornerShape(16.dp), dialog)
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme

+import android.graphics.Paint

+/**
+ * Tokkl's type scale (docs/PRD.md, Visual direction, Spacing and type scale): each named role's
+ * size and weight, and the font each Anybody role really draws with. Compose falls back to the
+ * nearest registered weight, so a 900 role with only the 800 instance registered draws at 800.
+ * Shaping a glyph with the resolved typeface reports the variable font's axes as drawn.
+ */
+class TypeScaleTest {
+    @get:Rule
+    val composeRule = createComposeRule()
+
+    @Test
+    fun namedRolesFollowTheScale() {
+        assertRole("page title", TokklTheme.pageTitleTextStyle, size = 28, weight = 900, letterSpacingEm = 0.02f)
+        assertRole("clock", TokklTheme.clockTextStyle, size = 36, weight = 800)
+        assertRole("totals", TokklTheme.totalTextStyle, size = 28, weight = 800)
+        assertRole("day total", TokklTheme.dayTotalTextStyle, size = 22, weight = 800)
+        assertRole("day header", TokklTheme.dayHeaderTextStyle, size = 17, weight = 900)
+        assertRole("card title", TokklTheme.cardTitleTextStyle, size = 18, weight = 800)
+        assertRole("section heading", TokklTheme.sectionHeadingTextStyle, size = 16, weight = 800)
+        assertRole("button label", TokklTheme.buttonLabelTextStyle, size = 14, weight = 700)
+        assertEquals("tabular clock digits", "tnum", TokklTheme.clockTextStyle.fontFeatureSettings)
+        assertEquals("tabular total digits", "tnum", TokklTheme.totalTextStyle.fontFeatureSettings)
+        assertEquals("tabular day total digits", "tnum", TokklTheme.dayTotalTextStyle.fontFeatureSettings)
+        assertEquals("monospace family", FontFamily.Monospace, TokklTheme.monospaceTextStyle.fontFamily)
+
+        lateinit var typography: Typography
+        lateinit var smallCaps: TextStyle
+        composeRule.setContent {
+            TokklTheme {
+                typography = MaterialTheme.typography
+                smallCaps = TokklTheme.smallCapsLabelTextStyle
+            }
+        }
+        composeRule.waitForIdle()
+        assertRole("small-caps label", smallCaps, size = 12, weight = 700, letterSpacingEm = 0.06f)
+        assertSame("headlineLarge is the page title", TokklTheme.pageTitleTextStyle, typography.headlineLarge)
+        assertSame("titleMedium is the section heading", TokklTheme.sectionHeadingTextStyle, typography.titleMedium)
+        assertSame("labelLarge is the button label", TokklTheme.buttonLabelTextStyle, typography.labelLarge)
+        for ((name, style) in listOf("bodyLarge" to typography.bodyLarge, "bodyMedium" to typography.bodyMedium, "bodySmall" to typography.bodySmall)) {
+            assertEquals("$name weight", FontWeight.Normal, style.fontWeight)
+        }
+        assertEquals("body sizes", listOf(16.sp, 14.sp, 12.sp), listOf(typography.bodyLarge, typography.bodyMedium, typography.bodySmall).map { it.fontSize })
+    }
+
+    @SdkSuppress(minSdkVersion = Build.VERSION_CODES.S)
+    @Test
+    fun anybodyRolesDrawAtTheirWeightAndWidth85() {
+        val drawn = mutableMapOf<String, Map<String, Float>>()
+        val roles = mapOf(
+            "page title" to TokklTheme.pageTitleTextStyle,
+            "day header" to TokklTheme.dayHeaderTextStyle,
+            "clock" to TokklTheme.clockTextStyle,
+            "section heading" to TokklTheme.sectionHeadingTextStyle,
+        )
+        composeRule.setContent {
+            val resolver = LocalFontFamilyResolver.current
+            for ((name, style) in roles) {
+                val typeface = resolver.resolve(style.fontFamily, checkNotNull(style.fontWeight)).value as Typeface
+                drawn[name] = axesOf(typeface)
+            }
+        }
+        composeRule.waitForIdle()
+
+        for ((name, style) in roles) {
+            val axes = drawn.getValue(name)
+            assertEquals("$name weight as drawn", checkNotNull(style.fontWeight).weight.toFloat(), axes["wght"])
+            assertEquals("$name width as drawn", ANYBODY_WIDTH, axes["wdth"])
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun assertRole(name: String, style: TextStyle, size: Int, weight: Int, letterSpacingEm: Float? = null) {
+        assertEquals("$name size", size.sp, style.fontSize)
+        assertEquals("$name weight", FontWeight(weight), style.fontWeight)
+        if (letterSpacingEm != null) assertEquals("$name tracking", letterSpacingEm.em, style.letterSpacing)
+    }
+
+    /** The variation axes of the font that draws "A" in [typeface]. */
+    @androidx.annotation.RequiresApi(Build.VERSION_CODES.S)
+    private fun axesOf(typeface: Typeface): Map<String, Float> {
+        val paint = Paint().apply { this.typeface = typeface }
+        val glyphs = TextRunShaper.shapeTextRun("A", 0, 1, 0, 1, 0f, 0f, false, paint)
+        return glyphs.getFont(0).axes.orEmpty().associate { it.tag to it.styleValue }
+    }
+}

+     * Like [send], and keeps the response's headers too, for calls that put
+     * values in them, such as the Reports API's `X-Next-Row-Number`, the row
+     * the next page of a detailed report starts at.
+     */
+    fun <T> sendWithHeaders(
+        method: String,
+        url: HttpUrl,
+        body: JsonElement?,
+        deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>,
+    ): ApiResult<WithHeaders<T>> = execute(method, url, body) { response ->
+        when (val decoded = decode(response.body.string(), deserializer)) {
+            is ApiResult.Success -> ApiResult.Success(WithHeaders(decoded.value, response.headers))
+            is ApiResult.Failure -> decoded
+        }
+    }
+
     /**
      * Like [send], for calls whose response body the caller does not need,
      * such as Tokkl's DELETE of a time entry, task, client or tag, which
@@ -71,10 +89,10 @@ class TogglApiClient(

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import kotlinx.serialization.SerialName
+import kotlinx.serialization.Serializable
+
+// The Toggl API v9 shapes the cache reads, as Tokkl and Toggl Track send them. Only the fields the
+// app uses are here; the decoder skips the rest. Fields that either server may send as null or
+// leave out are nullable, so one odd row cannot fail the whole answer.
+
+/** A client from `GET /api/v9/me?with_related_data=true`. Toggl names its workspace `wid`. */
+@Serializable
+class ServerClient(
+    val id: Long,
+    @SerialName("wid") val workspaceId: Long,
+    val name: String? = null,
+    val archived: Boolean? = null,
+)
+
+/** A project. [active] is false for an archived project. */
+@Serializable
+class ServerProject(
+    val id: Long,
+    @SerialName("workspace_id") val workspaceId: Long,
+    @SerialName("client_id") val clientId: Long? = null,
+    val name: String? = null,
+    val color: String? = null,
+    val active: Boolean? = null,
+    val billable: Boolean? = null,
+)
+
+/** A tag. Neither Tokkl nor Toggl archives tags. */
+@Serializable
+class ServerTag(
+    val id: Long,
+    @SerialName("workspace_id") val workspaceId: Long,
+    val name: String? = null,
+)
+
+/** A task. [active] is false for a task marked done. */
+@Serializable
+class ServerTask(
+    val id: Long,
+    @SerialName("workspace_id") val workspaceId: Long,
+    @SerialName("project_id") val projectId: Long? = null,
+    val name: String? = null,
+    val active: Boolean? = null,
+)
+
+/**
+ * A time entry from `GET /api/v9/me/time_entries`. [start] and [stop] are RFC 3339 timestamps,
+ * and [stop] is null while the entry runs.
+ */
+@Serializable
+class ServerTimeEntry(
+    val id: Long,
+    @SerialName("workspace_id") val workspaceId: Long,
+    @SerialName("project_id") val projectId: Long? = null,
+    @SerialName("task_id") val taskId: Long? = null,
+    val description: String? = null,
+    val start: String,
+    val stop: String? = null,
+    val billable: Boolean? = null,
+    val tags: List<String>? = null,
+    @SerialName("tag_ids") val tagIds: List<Long>? = null,
+)
+
+/** `GET /api/v9/me/preferences`. Tokkl leaves `favorites` out until the user saves one. */
+@Serializable
+class ServerPreferences(
+    val favorites: List<ServerFavorite>? = null,
+)

new file mode 100644

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.cache
+
+import android.database.sqlite.SQLiteException
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.capabilities.Capabilities
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Me
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.MePreferences
+import java.time.Clock
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.Flow
+
+/**
+ * What the app last loaded from the server, readable offline (docs/PRD.md O2): projects, clients,
+ * tags, tasks, favorites and about two weeks of history. Screens read the flows, which come from
+ * the phone and never wait for the network. [refresh] replaces the rows with the server's values.
+ *
+ * The account settings are not cached here. AccountSettingsSource keeps them and stays their one
+ * source of truth.
+ */
+class AccountCache(private val database: CacheDatabase, private val clock: Clock = Clock.systemUTC()) {
+
+    private val dao = database.cache()
+
+    /** The cached history, newest entry first. */
+    val timeEntries: Flow<List<CachedTimeEntry>> = dao.timeEntries()
+    val projects: Flow<List<CachedProject>> = dao.projects()
+    val clients: Flow<List<CachedClient>> = dao.clients()
+    val tags: Flow<List<CachedTag>> = dao.tags()
+    val tasks: Flow<List<CachedTask>> = dao.tasks()
+
+    /** In the user's order. */
+    val favorites: Flow<List<CachedFavorite>> = dao.favorites()
+
+    /**
+     * Reads the server with [api] and replaces the cached rows, as [refreshedWith] describes.
+     * [me] is the refresh's `GET /api/v9/me` with related data, [preferences] its
+     * `GET /api/v9/me/preferences`, null when that failed, and [capabilities] the flags they set.
+     * A server without favorites gets none cached. A failed request keeps its rows, and a failed
+     * write keeps the whole cache as it was. Blocks, so call it off the main thread.
+     */
+    fun refresh(api: TogglApiClient, me: Me, preferences: MePreferences?, capabilities: Capabilities) {
+        val snapshot = readServerSnapshot(api, me, preferences, clock, favorites = capabilities.favorites)
+        try {
+            dao.refresh { it.refreshedWith(snapshot) }
+        } catch (e: SQLiteException) {
+            // Keeps the cache until the next refresh.
+        }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Deletes every cached row. Signing out calls this, together with clearing the stored
+     * session and settings. Blocks, so call it off the main thread.
+     */
+    fun clear() {
+        database.clearAllTables()
+    }
+}

new file mode 100644
