@@ -76,6 +76,12 @@ Decisions:
 - **Components.** Material 3 through Compose Material 3, the official Material toolkit for Jetpack Compose.
 - **Colour.** Always Tokkl's palette, never the wallpaper. Light theme: sand background, ink text, sunset orange primary, aqua secondary, hot pink and lime for states. Dark theme: navy background with the neon counterparts. The source of truth is the colour tokens in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css`. Dynamic color is off, at most an opt-in setting.
 - **Type.** Anybody for the running clock, totals and headings, bundled with the app. Tokkl already ships it under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (`../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/OFL.txt`). Everything else uses the system font.
+- **Spacing and type scale.** Spacing is 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32 and 48dp, Tokkl's `--space-1` to `--space-8`. Screens keep a 16dp margin on compact widths and 24dp on medium and wider, and content is capped at 600dp. Anybody, always at width 85, sets the page title (28sp, weight 900, uppercase), clock (36sp, 800), totals (28sp, 800), day total (22sp, 800), day header (17sp, 900, uppercase), card title (18sp, 800) and section heading (16sp, 800). The system font sets body text at 16, 14 and 12sp, the small-caps label at 12sp weight 700 and the button label at 14sp weight 700. A monospace role sets fingerprints and codes.
+- **Wordmark.** The owner's graffiti artwork, shipped as an image with the content description "Tokkl" for TalkBack, so it uses no font. In dark mode a 1dp halo in `--wordmark-halo` (#A9B0D6) traces its edge, because the artwork's navy outline matches the background there. Light mode draws no halo. See `../tokkl/docs/PRD.md`, Visual direction, Typeface.
+- **Texture.** Gradients or vectors only, never raster images, and never behind inputs or data. See `../tokkl/docs/PRD.md`, Visual direction, Texture.
+- **Type effects.** Rotated or skewed type is decoration only. It never appears on controls, numbers or labels. See `../tokkl/docs/PRD.md`, Visual direction, Type effects.
+- **Motion.** All motion respects the system's remove-animations setting. With it on, nothing animates. See `../tokkl/docs/PRD.md`, Visual direction, Motion.
+- **Bundled assets.** Fonts and images ship with the app. The app loads none from outside, which follows principle 1. See `../tokkl/docs/PRD.md`, Visual direction, No outbound assets.
 - **Contrast and signals.** WCAG AA, as in Tokkl: 4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for non-text elements. Colour is never the only signal for a state.
 - **Devices.** Phones first. Layouts adapt to tablets and foldables, with no separate design.

+# A run killed with SIGKILL, or ended along with the session that started it,
+# skips the EXIT trap and leaves its credentials file on the device. This
+# deletes those files on device $1 once they are STALE_CREDENTIALS_HOURS old,
+# so a file that another run on the same device still reads stays, then syncs
+# as the cleanup does. A device whose find rejects toybox's +6h syntax keeps
+# its files and gets a warning.
+remove_stale_credentials() {
+    local device=$1 removed
+    if removed=$(adb -s "$device" shell \
+        "find $CREDENTIALS_DIR -maxdepth 1 -name '$CREDENTIALS_PREFIX*.properties' -mtime +${STALE_CREDENTIALS_HOURS}h -print -delete && sync"); then
+        if [ -n "$removed" ]; then
+            echo "Removed stale credentials files from $device: ${removed//$'\n'/ }"
+        fi
+    else
+        echo "$0: could not look for stale credentials files on $device" >&2
+    fi
+}
+

     adb devices | awk 'NR > 1 && $2 == "device" { print $1 }'
 }

description: >-
  No minimum Tokkl version. A missing /me field or a 404 from a Tokkl-only
  endpoint hides the features that depend on it.
tags:
  - api
  - toggl
  - compatibility
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-check-capabilities-not-server-version
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app sets no minimum server version and has no version coupling with Tokkl. It checks what the server can do instead.

A missing `time_resolution_minutes` in `GET /api/v9/me` means there is no snapping to show. A 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint such as `/api/app/trash` hides the features that use it.

The same checks hide the Tokkl-only features against Toggl Track later: trash, undo of a delete, favorites and time resolution. Older Tokkl servers get the same treatment.

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling.md
---
type: practice
title: 'Refresh on events, never on a short polling timer'
description: >-
  Refresh when the app opens, after a change and when the network returns. No
  short polling, so Toggl's base URL later fits its quotas.
tags:
  - network
  - toggl
  - battery
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app makes few requests. It refreshes the running entry and the visible data when it opens, after a change, and when the network comes back. It does not poll on a short timer. The running clock ticks on the phone from the start time.

Toggl's quotas are the reason. A later version points the app at Toggl's base URL. Toggl caps workspace and organization requests per user per organization at 30 an hour on Free, 240 on Starter and 600 on Premium. User endpoints such as `/me` are capped at 30 an hour on every plan below Enterprise. The window slides over 60 minutes, and a request over the limit gets HTTP 402. Tokkl's web app polls the running timer every 30 seconds, which alone is 120 requests an hour. An app that polled like that would hit the Free quota within 15 minutes.

Battery follows the same rule. The app makes no wakeups while no timer runs and the queue is empty.

<!-- kk:citations:start -->

[1] [docs/PRD.md](../../../docs/PRD.md)

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop.md
---
type: practice
title: Never make Start or Stop wait for the server
description: >-
  Taps change state on the phone at once and queue the change. The server is the
  source of truth, with a cache and queue, no sync engine.
tags:
  - offline
  - network
  - api
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Tapping Start or Stop never fails because the server is out of reach. A tap in the app, notification, widget or tile changes the state on the phone without waiting for the server. The notification, widget and tile read the running entry from the phone, so they react at once, and the queue sends the change behind them.

Each offline change waits in a queue on the phone, marked as not sent, and goes out in order when the server is reachable. A timer started offline keeps its real start time. The queue sends in the background when the network comes back, even with the app closed. The app shows how many changes are not sent and when the server last answered (O1 to O5). Some phone makers restrict background work, which can delay sending the queue.

The app has no sync engine and no full local copy of the account. The server is the source of truth. The phone keeps a cache of what it last loaded and the queue of unsent changes. The cache holds projects, clients, tags, tasks, favorites and about two weeks of history, all readable and editable offline. When the phone and another client both changed an entry, the change that reaches the server last wins. Signing out warns when unsent changes would be lost, then clears the cache and the queue.

Reports and history older than the cache need the server. With no connection the reports screen says so instead of showing stale numbers (R5).

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md
---
type: practice
title: Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone
description: >-
  Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account
  timezone read from GET /api/v9/me.
tags:
  - api
  - ui
  - time
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics
  - map-account-settings-source-and-account-time
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app uses the same time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo as Tokkl's web app, in the account's timezone. Moving between the laptop and the phone then needs no relearning.

Read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`, and use the account timezone everywhere (S7). Resolve its name with `time.parseTimeZone`, never `ZoneId.of`. Tokkl accepts what JavaScript's Intl accepts, such as `europe/madrid` in any case and ICU-only names like `IST`, and rejects some names `ZoneId.of` takes, such as `Z` or `GMT+5`. A name it cannot resolve gives null. Never fall back to the device's zone. Screens take the zone, the clock and the dates from `AccountTime`, and `NoDeviceZoneTest` fails on any read of the device's zone (see the account settings node). Show stopped entries as the server returns them, after it snaps them to `time_resolution_minutes` (T3). The phone snaps entries it has not sent yet in the account timezone, so their times do not change when the server answers. History and queue code calls `snapUnsent` in `time/Snap.kt`, never `snapSpan` directly. The server cuts both ends to whole seconds before it snaps (`snapToResolution` in `../tokkl/packages/api/src/models/timeEntries.ts`), and `snapUnsent` does the same. With no `time_resolution_minutes` in `/me` it returns the span unchanged. Never pass it the running entry, whose clock is not snapped.

Manual entry accepts times like `9`, `930`, `9:30` and `4pm`, entries across midnight, and durations like `1h 30m`, `90` and `1,5h` (M1). The parsing is a Kotlin port of Tokkl's `parseDuration` in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts` and `parseTimeOfDay` in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts`, checked against the cases in the `.test.ts` file next to each. The time-of-day parser lives in the web package, not in `packages/common`. Both ports live in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time`, and their tests copy the TypeScript cases verbatim at a recorded tokkl commit (see the porting practice node). The ports can still drift when the TypeScript changes later. Sharing the test cases between the two repos would catch that.

Favorites are the same ones the web app shows, read from and written to `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

# Start every instrumented run on the test emulator at font scale 1.0

## Problem

The tokkl-api36 emulator keeps the system font scale at 2.0 across boots. Every instrumented class that does not set its own scale runs at 200%, so a screen test meant for the default size checks the largest size instead. Screenshots taken at the default scale also come out at 200%. SystemFontScale puts back whatever value it read before the test, so once one run leaves 2.0 behind, later runs keep restoring it. The script resets other settings on an emulator it boots, but not the font scale.

## Evidence

- On 2026-10-18, a disposable probe read `settings get system font_scale` before each of its tests, on three separate boots of tokkl-api36 through the script. Every read gave `2.0`, and the probe put that value back afterwards.
- app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/SystemFontScale.kt:17 reads the current scale as `previous`, and :26 writes `previous` back after the test, whatever it was.
- scripts/instrumented-tests.sh:171-172 sets `hide_error_dialogs` and closes system dialogs on an emulator the script boots. It sets nothing about the font scale.

## Direction

On an emulator it boots, have scripts/instrumented-tests.sh delete the system font scale setting, or set it to 1.0, before Gradle runs, next to the `hide_error_dialogs` step. It keeps changing no setting on a device it did not boot. Tests that need another scale keep setting it themselves through SystemFontScale. Update the kenkeep node on running instrumented tests through the script if it lists what the script sets on the emulator.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] After the script boots tokkl-api36, `settings get system font_scale` reads `null` or `1.0` before the first test runs, even when the AVD was left at 2.0.
- [ ] A device the script did not boot keeps its font scale.
- [ ] LargestFontScaleTest and ClockAndTotalStylesFitTest still run at 2.0 and pass.

On tokkl-api36, focusing a sign-in field and typing into it never opens the soft keyboard: the window's keyboard inset stays at 0. LargestFontScaleTest waits for the keyboard to hide before it measures the trust prompts, on the assumption that typing opened it, so that wait passes at once. No test sees the sign-in form with the keyboard open, so the IME padding TKA-0105 kept has no test coverage.

On later boots `settings get secure show_ime_with_hard_keyboard` read 0 before the probe changed it. With the setting at 1, the same steps in light gave a 796 px keyboard inset.

[credential-related line omitted]

- [ ] A test fails when the focused sign-in field sits under the keyboard, and passes now.

# Draw edge to edge in both themes with no navigation bar scrim

## Problem

With three-button navigation, the system draws a near-white band under the sand screen in light mode. Its pixels read 254,253,250, which is exactly what a 0xE6 white scrim gives over the sand background, and dark mode shows a lighter navy band. The comment at MainActivity.kt:78 says those scrims draw only on Android 9 and earlier, so the comment and the screen disagree. Each screen also applies the scaffold's `innerPadding` outside `verticalScroll`, so scrolled text is cut off at the bar's top edge instead of passing under it. The owner approved fixing this after seeing the screenshots.

## Expected behaviour

In light and dark mode the navigation bar area shows the screen's own background. Scrolling content passes under a transparent bar, and the bar's inset becomes padding inside the scrolling content.

## Steps to reproduce

1. On an API 36 device or emulator, switch the system to three-button navigation.
2. Open the app in light mode and sample a pixel in the navigation bar area.
3. Switch the app to dark mode in Settings and sample the same pixel.
4. Open Licences and scroll to the end. The text stops at the top edge of the navigation bar.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:41 calls `enableEdgeToEdge()` with its defaults before `setContent`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:71 calls `enableEdgeToEdge` again with `navigationBarStyle = SystemBarStyle.auto(LIGHT_SCRIM, DARK_SCRIM)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:77-81 sets `LIGHT_SCRIM` to `argb(0xe6, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF)` and `DARK_SCRIM` to `argb(0x80, 0x1b, 0x1b, 0x1b)`, under a comment at :78 saying only Android 9 and earlier draw them.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/settings/SettingsScreen.kt:68 applies `innerPadding` before `verticalScroll` at :69.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:41 applies `innerPadding` before `verticalScroll` at :42.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:134 applies `innerPadding` before `imePadding` at :136 and `verticalScroll` at :137.

## Direction

Find out which mechanism paints the band before changing anything. It is either the scrim that `enableEdgeToEdge` passes at MainActivity.kt:71, or the system's navigation bar contrast enforcement, which `window.isNavigationBarContrastEnforced = false` turns off. Fix it at the cause, keep or drop `LIGHT_SCRIM` and `DARK_SCRIM` at MainActivity.kt:77-81 to match, and rewrite the comment at MainActivity.kt:78 so it states the real cause. MainActivity.kt:41 runs before the theme is known, so check that the first frame shows no band either.

On sign-in, Settings and Licences, take the system-bar insets out of the outer padding and give them to the scrolling content as content padding. Text then scrolls under the transparent bar, and the last line can still scroll clear of it. Sign-in keeps its IME padding. Keep `consumeWindowInsets` at SignInScreen.kt:135, or an equivalent, so `imePadding` does not add the navigation bar inset twice. The status bar icons keep following the chosen theme mode, as `SystemBarStyle.auto` at MainActivity.kt:70 does now. Do not change any theme colour.

TKA-0107 changes the padding on the same column modifiers, so whichever ticket lands second keeps both changes.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] With three-button navigation on API 36, the pixels under the navigation bar match the screen background in light and dark mode, checked with a screenshot or a pixel sample.
- [ ] On Licences, scrolled text passes under the navigation bar and the last line can scroll fully above it.
- [ ] Settings and sign-in pass scrolled content under the navigation bar the same way, and sign-in still keeps the focused field above the keyboard.
- [ ] The comment at MainActivity.kt:78 states the real cause.
- [ ] Gesture navigation still shows no band.

# Render the OFL readably, at body size and with separators that do not wrap

## Problem

The OFL is the longest text in the app and the only one set in `bodySmall`, 12sp, which is a caption size. The Licences screen shows it as one proportional `Text` with soft wrap. Each long dashed separator line therefore wraps onto a stray second line of dashes. The owner approved fixing this after seeing the screenshots.

## Expected behaviour

The licence reads at body size, and each separator line shows as a single rule at every width and font scale.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:33-36 reads res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt into one string and only turns CRLF into LF.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:70 shows that string as one `Text` in `MaterialTheme.typography.bodySmall`.

## Direction

Set the licence text in `bodyMedium`. Replace the OFL's dashed separator lines with a `HorizontalDivider` at render time, by splitting the text read at LicencesScreen.kt:33-36 on lines made only of dashes, or show those lines in a style that cannot wrap. res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt:8 and :10 are the two separators, 59 dashes each. Keep res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt byte-identical, since the OFL must ship unchanged.

Keep the existing Licences tests passing. LicencesScreenTest.kt:35-42 finds the one node whose text contains the OFL title and compares its lines, dashes included, with the shipped file. Splitting the text across several `Text` nodes fails that check unless one node still exposes the full text to semantics.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The licence text uses `bodyMedium`.
- [ ] No dashed separator wraps onto a second line at 1x or the largest font scale.
- [ ] res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt is unchanged.
- [ ] LicencesScreenTest still passes.

# Pass Tokkl's shapes to MaterialTheme so fields and cards drop Material's default corners

## Problem

Theme.kt passes no shapes to `MaterialTheme`, so every component keeps Material's default corners. `OutlinedTextField` draws a 4dp corner and a 1dp outline, while Tokkl's inputs use a 10px radius (`--radius-md`) and its cards a 16px radius (`--radius-lg`) with a 2px frame. In the screenshots the sign-in fields are the most stock-Material part of the screen.

## Expected behaviour

Fields, cards and dialogs use Tokkl's corner radii and frame widths: 10dp corners for fields, and 16dp corners with a 2dp frame for cards and dialogs.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Theme.kt:40 calls `MaterialTheme` with a colour scheme, typography and content, and no `shapes`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:146-165 draws the server URL field as an `OutlinedTextField` with no shape of its own.

## Direction

Define a `Shapes` set from these radii and pass it to `MaterialTheme` at Theme.kt:40. Give text fields the 10dp radius. This covers the server URL field at SignInScreen.kt:146-165 and the other sign-in fields, which are `OutlinedTextField`s too (SignInScreen.kt:182 and :266). Give them a 2dp focused outline where Material allows it through colours and shapes. Give cards and dialogs the 16dp radius. `Shapes` sets corners only, so the 2dp frame on cards and dialogs needs each component's own border where it has one. Keep 3:1 non-text contrast for outlines, as ThemeContrastTest checks.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `MaterialTheme` receives Tokkl shapes.
- [ ] The sign-in fields draw with 10dp corners.
- [ ] Dialogs and cards use 16dp corners.
- [ ] ThemeContrastTest still passes.

[credential-related line omitted]

## Direction

Give the email field a supporting-text slot that is always present, as the secret field does at :270 and the server URL field does at SignInScreen.kt:150-156. Fill it with a hint from a new English string resource or with an empty line, and show the error in the same slot. Keep `isError` tied to the error so the field's error semantics still announce it to TalkBack.

## Acceptance criteria

[credential-related line omitted]
- [ ] The error is still announced to TalkBack.
- [ ] SignInScreenTest passes through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh.

resolution: "MainActivity uses adjustResize; focusedSignInFieldStaysAboveTheKeyboard asserts the compose root sits at y=0. Root was at -258 px before and 0 after; with imePadding removed the test now fails (1862 > 1604). No criteria waived."
---

# Stop Android panning the sign-in window when the keyboard opens

## Problem

When the soft keyboard opens on sign-in, Android pans the whole window up 258 px, and the form's IME padding shrinks its scrolling area as well. So the keyboard is handled twice. The top 258 px of the window go off the screen, and a 258 px gap opens between the focused field and the keyboard. The pan also keeps the focused field in view without the IME padding, so LargestFontScaleTest's keyboard test passes with the padding removed and does not cover it.

## Expected behaviour

The window stays in place when the keyboard opens. Only the form's IME padding makes room, so the scrolling area ends at the keyboard's top and the focused field sits just above it.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:26-29 declares MainActivity with no `android:windowSoftInputMode`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:133 applies `imePadding()` to the form.
- On 2026-10-18 a disposable diagnostic in `LargestFontScaleTest.focusedSignInFieldStaysAboveTheKeyboard` (app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/LargestFontScaleTest.kt:186) measured on tokkl-api36 at font scale 2.0, with `show_ime_with_hard_keyboard` at 1. Positions are window pixels, and the keyboard's top was at 1604 px in every run:
[credential-related line omitted]
  - With `imePadding()` removed, the window was again panned by 258 px and the field ended at 1604 px, so the test passed.
  - With `android:windowSoftInputMode="adjustResize"` on MainActivity, the decor view sat at 0 and the field ended at 1604 px.
  - With `adjustResize` and no `imePadding()`, the field stayed at 1862 px and the test failed.
- LargestFontScaleTest.kt:179-182 records that the pan alone keeps the field in view.

## Direction

Set `android:windowSoftInputMode="adjustResize"` on MainActivity, so the window stays in place and only the insets reach Compose. Then extend `focusedSignInFieldStaysAboveTheKeyboard` to check that the window did not pan, for example that the compose root's `positionInWindow.y` is 0 with the keyboard open. Then the test fails without the form's IME padding. Update the test's comment about the pan. Settings and Licences have no text fields today, so they need no change.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] With the keyboard open on sign-in, the window is not panned: the compose root sits at y = 0 in the window.
- [ ] The focused sign-in field ends at or above the keyboard's top.
- [ ] `focusedSignInFieldStaysAboveTheKeyboard` fails with `imePadding()` removed from the sign-in form, and passes with it.

LicenceReflowTest 7/7 over the shipped file (4 fail with the reflow removed); LicencesScreenTest 4/4; before/after screenshots at 1.0 and 2.0 reviewed. No criteria waived."
---

# Reflow the licence's hard-wrapped lines so sentences do not break mid-line

## Problem

res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt is the upstream OFL.txt, hard-wrapped at about 75 characters with CRLF line ends. The Licences screen keeps every one of those line breaks. At bodyMedium on a phone a line holds about 45 characters, so each source line wraps once and then breaks early, and the text alternates long and short lines with sentences cut in odd places, such as "to support the font creation" followed by a line starting "efforts of academic". TKA-0106 moved the text to body size and turned the dashed rules into dividers, but it kept the source's line breaks, so the licence is still hard to read.

## Expected behaviour

Each paragraph of the licence flows as one block that wraps at the screen's width. Blank lines still separate paragraphs, the numbered conditions 1) to 5) still start on their own lines, and the dividers from TKA-0106 stay where they are.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt has 93 lines, most of them 70 to 77 characters long, ending in CRLF. Lines 14 to 17 split one sentence across "…to support the font creation", "efforts of academic and linguistic communities, and to provide a free and" and "open framework…".
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:93-106 splits the text at the rule lines only and passes each block to `Text` with its inner line breaks intact.
- The w6-e2 screenshots /tmp/sift-w6-e2/shots/after/after-licences-mid-three-light.png and after-licences-top-three-light.png, taken at font scale 1.0 on 2026-10-18, show the ragged breaks in the PREAMBLE and PERMISSION & CONDITIONS sections.

## Direction

- Leave res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt unchanged; reflow at display time.
- Within each block, normalise CRLF, then join lines that belong to one paragraph with a space. Keep blank lines as paragraph breaks.
- Keep a line break before a line that starts a numbered condition, such as `1)`, and before the first line after the heading lines, so headings like "PREAMBLE" and "DEFINITIONS" stay on their own lines. Check the result against the whole file, not only the preamble.
- Keep one semantics node with the full licence text, as TKA-0106 does.
- Put the reflow in a pure function with a JVM test over the shipped file.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] At font scale 1.0 and 2.0, no paragraph of the licence breaks a line before the screen's width except at a paragraph end, a heading or a numbered condition.
- [ ] res/raw/anybody_ofl.txt is unchanged.
- [ ] A JVM test runs the reflow over the shipped file and checks that it keeps every word in order and keeps the headings and numbered conditions on their own lines.
- [ ] LicencesScreenTest still passes.

# Carry Tokkl's texture, wordmark, type-effect, motion and bundled-asset rules into this PRD

## Problem

Principle 5 of docs/PRD.md applies Tokkl's sixth principle as written. This PRD's Visual direction lists only components, colour, type, contrast and devices. An agent that reads this repository alone never learns the rules the design tickets rely on: texture only as gradients, the wordmark as an image, rotated type only as decoration, motion that respects reduced motion, and no outbound assets. Those rules live only in Tokkl's PRD.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:72 is principle 5, "Loud look, plain function", which applies Tokkl's sixth principle as written.
- docs/PRD.md:76-80 is the Visual direction, with bullets for components, colour, type, contrast and signals, and devices.

## Direction

Add bullets to the Visual direction in docs/PRD.md for these rules:

- Wordmark. The owner's graffiti artwork, shipped as an image named "Tokkl" for TalkBack, with a 1dp halo in `--wordmark-halo` (#A9B0D6) around it in dark mode.
- Texture. Gradients or vectors only, never raster images, and never behind inputs or data.
- Type effects. Rotated or skewed type only as decoration, never on controls, numbers or labels.
- Motion. All motion respects the system's remove-animations setting.
- Bundled assets. Fonts and images ship with the app, with no outbound fonts or images.

Point each bullet to the matching bullet of the Visual direction in ../tokkl/docs/PRD.md by heading and bullet name (Typeface, Texture, Type effects, Motion, No outbound assets), not by line number.

Add one short bullet that records the spacing and type scale. Spacing is 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32 and 48dp (Tokkl's `--space-1` to `--space-8`), with a 16dp screen margin on compact widths, 24dp on medium and wider, and content capped at 600dp. Anybody, always at width 85, sets the page title (28sp, weight 900, uppercase), clock (36sp, 800), totals (28sp, 800), day total (22sp, 800), day header (17sp, 900, uppercase), card title (18sp, 800) and section heading (16sp, 800). The system font sets body text at 16, 14 and 12sp, the small-caps label at 12sp weight 700 and the button label at 14sp weight 700. A monospace role sets fingerprints and codes. Do not change other sections.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The Visual direction states the wordmark, texture, type-effect, motion and bundled-asset rules.
- [ ] It records the spacing and type scale in one short bullet.
- [ ] References to Tokkl use headings, not line numbers.
- [ ] Principle 5 and every section outside the Visual direction are unchanged.

# Extract the shared selectable option row used by sign-in, Settings and the http warning

## Problem

The sign-in method radios, the Settings theme radios and the http warning's checkbox each build the same row by hand. Every copy is a full-width Row at least 48dp tall, clickable across its width with a radio or checkbox role, holding the control, a 12dp spacer and the label. A change to the spacing or the semantics has to be made three times, and the copies can drift apart.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:237 builds the sign-in method row, 48dp minimum and `selectable` with `Role.RadioButton`, with a 12dp spacer before the label.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/settings/SettingsScreen.kt:106 builds the theme radio row the same way.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:307-317 builds the http warning's checkbox row, `toggleable` with `Role.Checkbox`, also 48dp with a 12dp spacer.

## Direction

Add one option-row composable in `ui/`. It can offer a radio variant and a checkbox variant, or take the control as a slot. The row fills the width, has a 48dp minimum height and is clickable across its width, with `selectable` and `Role.RadioButton` for radios and `toggleable` and `Role.Checkbox` for the checkbox. The gap between control and label is the spacing scale's 12dp step, Tokkl's --space-3. Keep the controls' own click handlers null, as SignInScreen.kt:244, SignInScreen.kt:314 and SettingsScreen.kt:117 do today, so TalkBack reads each row as one item. Keep `selectableGroup` on the radio groups' parent columns. Use the row at all three sites and keep the existing tests passing unchanged.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The sign-in method radios, the Settings theme radios and the http warning checkbox all use the shared row.
- [ ] Each row is 48dp minimum and clickable across its width with a radio or checkbox role.
- [ ] SignInScreenTest, including its http warning tests, and SettingsScreenTest pass through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh.

# Add a spacing scale, one screen margin and a content width cap, and use them on every screen

## Problem

Sign-in and home pad 24dp on every side, while Settings and Licences pad 16dp across and 8dp down. The http warning and the certificate prompt space their content 16dp and 8dp apart. The theme names no spacing values, so every screen picks its own literals and the content edge jumps from screen to screen. No screen caps its width either. On the tablets and foldables the PRD names, the sign-in form and the Settings rows stretch across the whole window.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:92 pads the home screen with `padding(24.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:138 pads sign-in with `padding(24.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/settings/SettingsScreen.kt:70 pads Settings with `padding(horizontal = 16.dp, vertical = 8.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:43 pads Licences with `padding(horizontal = 16.dp, vertical = 8.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:304 spaces the http warning with `Arrangement.spacedBy(16.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:353 spaces the certificate prompt with `Arrangement.spacedBy(8.dp)`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInScreen.kt:130-140 builds the sign-in column with `fillMaxSize()`, no width limit and `spacedBy(16.dp)` between fields.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/settings/SettingsScreen.kt:66 starts the Settings column's modifier chain, which has no width limit.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/licences/LicencesScreen.kt:39 starts the Licences column's modifier chain, which has no width limit.
- docs/PRD.md:80 says layouts adapt to tablets and foldables.
- absent: app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Spacing.kt. ui/theme holds only Color.kt, Theme.kt and Type.kt.

## Direction

Add ui/theme/Spacing.kt with Tokkl's spacing scale, taken from `--space-1` to `--space-8` in ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css: 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32 and 48dp. Expose it through the theme object next to `TokklTheme.extraColors`. Name the rhythm on top of the scale: 24dp between groups, 8dp inside a group and 16dp under the top bar.

Define one screen margin, 16dp on compact widths and 24dp on medium and wider, using Material's window size class breakpoints. Define a 600dp content width cap that centres the content on wider windows. Apply both on home, sign-in, Settings, Licences and both sign-in dialogs. The dialogs use the scale's in-group gap of 8dp and between-group gap of 24dp. Replace the literal dp values at the cited sites with the tokens.

This caps single-column content only. TKA-0056 still owns the navigation rail and multi-pane layouts. TKA-0105 moves the system-bar insets on the same column modifiers, so whichever ticket lands second keeps both changes.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] ui/theme/Spacing.kt defines the scale and the theme exposes it.
- [ ] Every cited site uses the tokens instead of literal dp values.
- [ ] Home, sign-in, Settings and Licences share one horizontal margin on a phone.
- [ ] On a window wider than 600dp plus margins, content is capped at 600dp and centred, checked by a test with a wide window size.
- [ ] Existing JVM tests pass, and instrumented tests pass through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh.

# Build the type scale from Tokkl's web ladder and register the Anybody 900 weight

Nothing matches the web's 28px totals, its 17px day headers or its uppercase page titles, and labels stay at Material's weight 500 where the web uses 600 to 800. The certificate fingerprint sets a monospace font directly instead of using a type role.

- Card title: 18, Anybody 800.

- Button label: 14, system font, weight 700.

# Add Tokkl's texture, scene and wordmark-halo colour tokens to the theme

## Problem

The brief's hand-made texture comes from six tokens in Tokkl's styles.css. `--texture-glow`, `--texture-swell` and `--texture-stripe` sit behind the web sidebar and the phone header strip, and `--scene-glow`, `--scene-swell` and `--scene-stripe` sit behind the auth page and the splash. The wordmark adds a seventh, `--wordmark-halo`. Color.kt maps none of them, so the top bar strip and the sign-in scene cannot be drawn in tested colours.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Color.kt:14-33 lists the styles.css tokens the app uses, and none of the seven is among them.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Color.kt:154-161 defines `TokklExtraColors`, the colours with no Material role, which holds only running, billable and the lime accent.

## Direction

Add light and dark values for `--texture-glow`, `--texture-swell`, `--texture-stripe`, `--scene-glow`, `--scene-swell`, `--scene-stripe` and `--wordmark-halo` from ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css to Color.kt, and name each token in the comment beside its value. The light `--wordmark-halo` is `transparent`, because only dark mode draws the halo. styles.css computes the texture and scene values by mixing primary and secondary over surface or bg, and stores the results as hex.

Expose the seven colours through the theme next to the state colours in `TokklExtraColors`. Add no drawing yet; TKA-0121, TKA-0123 and TKA-0124 use these tokens. Textures stay gradients, never raster images.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Each of the seven tokens has a light and a dark value equal to styles.css, named by token in a comment.
- [ ] The theme exposes them for composables.
- [ ] A JVM test checks the values against the hex in styles.css, or against a copied table that names its source.

---

# Set Tokkl's navigation bar item colours in the theme before the first NavigationBar

## Problem

Material's `NavigationBar` paints the active item's indicator in `secondaryContainer`, which Color.kt maps to `--state-selected`, a pale fill. Tokkl's web marks the active item as a primary block with an on-primary icon and label. The timer and history screens will add the first bottom bar. Unless the theme sets the item colours once, that bar will not look like Tokkl.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Color.kt:96 maps `secondaryContainer`, the default indicator colour, to `--state-selected`.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Color.kt:121 maps `surfaceContainer`, the bar's default container colour, to `--surface`.

## Direction

Add a theme-level `NavigationBarItemColors` default that later screens use, through a small composable or a defaults object, and keep Material 3's `NavigationBarItem`. The active indicator is a primary block behind the icon only, with the icon in on-primary. The active label sits below the indicator, on the bar's surface, in primary at weight 700, the button and label role weight. Inactive icons and labels use on-surface-variant, with labels at the normal label weight. The indicator shape and the bold label are the cues that do not depend on colour.

Tokkl's web puts the label inside the primary block (the `.nav-link.active` rule in ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css). Material 3's item layout draws the label below the indicator, on the bar's surface, where on-primary text would be unreadable. That is why the active label takes primary instead.

In ThemeContrastTest, check these pairs in light and dark. The bar surface is `surfaceContainer`, which Color.kt:121 maps to `--surface`.

- On-primary icon on the primary indicator, 3:1 for non-text.
- Primary active label on the bar surface, 4.5:1 for text.
- On-surface-variant inactive icon on the bar surface, 3:1.
- On-surface-variant inactive label on the bar surface, 4.5:1.

Do not add a `NavigationBar` to any screen. TKA-0056 and the screen tickets own navigation.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The theme provides Tokkl navigation bar item colours for `NavigationBarItem`. The active item has a primary indicator behind the icon, an on-primary icon, and a primary label at weight 700. Inactive items use on-surface-variant for icon and label.
- [ ] The default replaces `secondaryContainer` (Color.kt:96) as the active indicator colour without changing the colour scheme mapping.
- [ ] ThemeContrastTest checks all four pairs above in both themes, and they pass.
- [ ] No screen gains a navigation bar in this ticket.

# Ship the graffiti wordmark and icon as a Brand composable and use it on home and sign-in

## Problem

Tokkl's wordmark is the owner's graffiti artwork, and the web app ships it as an image named "Tokkl" in the brand block that opens every page. The app has no wordmark drawable at all. The home screen sets "Tokkl" as plain Anybody text with no heading semantics, so no screen shows the brand. In dark mode the artwork's navy outline also needs the halo the web draws around it, or the outline is lost against the navy background.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:96-100 sets the home title as the `app_name` string in `headlineLarge`, Anybody text with no heading semantics.
- absent: app/src/main/res/drawable-xxxhdpi/tokkl_wordmark.png does not exist, and that folder holds only the launcher icon layers.
- ../tokkl/scripts/icons.ts:17 defines Tokkl's wordmark crop, `WORDMARK_CROP = '1645x709+80+91'`.
- ../tokkl/scripts/icons.ts:57-64 derives the 640 px tokkl-wordmark.png and the web's wordmark-260.png and wordmark-520.png by cropping the 1774x887 ../tokkl/docs/brand/tokkl-wordmark-v3.png with that crop, then resizing.

## Direction

Derive the wordmark PNGs from ../tokkl/docs/brand/tokkl-wordmark-v3.png, read only, with Tokkl's own crop 1645x709+80+91, resized to the widths the 84, 130 and 260dp sizes need up to xxxhdpi. The largest is 260dp at 4x, 1040 px, which is under the crop's 1645 px, so nothing is upscaled. Do not start from the 640 px tokkl-wordmark.png, which is too small for the 260dp size at xxhdpi and xxxhdpi. Record the source, the crop and the steps next to the images, as TKA-0085 did for the icon in app/src/main/res/mipmap-anydpi/ic_launcher.xml:2-36. If the icon needs its own image rather than the launcher layers, record its source and steps the same way. Respect TKA-0111's bundled-assets rule.

Add a Brand composable that draws the icon beside the wordmark in three sizes, the icon at 24, 30 or 48dp with the wordmark at 84, 130 or 260dp wide. Name the wordmark "Tokkl" for TalkBack and keep the icon decorative, as the web's Brand component does. In dark mode draw a 1dp halo around the wordmark in TKA-0109's --wordmark-halo colour, which the web draws as four 1px drop shadows. Use the Brand on the home screen in place of the Anybody "Tokkl" text, and on the sign-in screen above the form. TKA-0124 builds the full sign-in scene around it.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The app ships wordmark images cut from tokkl-wordmark-v3.png with Tokkl's crop, with the source, the crop and the steps recorded beside them.
- [ ] The widest wordmark image is at least 1040 px wide, so the 260dp size is not upscaled at xxxhdpi.
- [ ] Home and sign-in show the Brand composable.
- [ ] TalkBack reads "Tokkl" once for the brand.
- [ ] In dark mode the halo keeps the wordmark's outline visible against navy, shown in a screenshot.
- [ ] Screenshot references are updated if present.

# Check text and wordmark-halo contrast on the texture and scene colours

## Problem

TKA-0109 added the texture, scene and wordmark-halo colours to the theme. ThemeContrastTest still checks no pair that uses them, so TKA-0121, TKA-0123 and TKA-0124 can put text or the wordmark on those gradients with no test behind the contrast. Tokkl's styles.css records these pairs in its contrast table, and the app's WCAG AA rule applies to them.

## Evidence

- app/src/test/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/ThemeContrastTest.kt:40 builds the pairs from the colour scheme and, through `x`, the running, billable and lime colours. No texture, scene or halo colour is among them.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Color.kt:179 defines `TokklExtraColors`, which holds the seven colours since TKA-0109.
- styles.css lists the rows "text on texture-glow", "muted on texture-glow" and the same for swell and stripe, "text on scene-glow", swell and stripe, and "wordmark-halo on bg, surface, texture and scene (worst)". The text rows need 4.5:1 and the halo row, of kind "other", needs 3:1.
- I computed every one of these pairs from the theme's values on 2026-10-18. The lowest text ratio is 4.69:1 (muted on texture-stripe, dark) and the lowest halo ratio is 4.69:1 (halo on texture-stripe, dark), so the tests pass as written.

## Direction

In ThemeContrastTest, add these pairs to both themes, taking the colours from `TokklExtraColors`:

- onSurface and onSurfaceVariant on `textureGlow`, `textureSwell` and `textureStripe`, as text (4.5:1).
- onSurface on `sceneGlow`, `sceneSwell` and `sceneStripe`, as text.
- In dark only, `wordmarkHalo` on background, surface, and each texture and scene colour, as non-text (3:1). The light halo is transparent and has no pair.

Do not add the "overlap (worst case)" rows. styles.css derives them from channel extremes where gradients overlap, and a drawing ticket owns that check.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] ThemeContrastTest checks the text pairs on the six texture and scene colours in light and dark.
- [ ] It checks the wordmark halo on background, surface and the six colours in dark.
- [ ] The tests pass with the current values.

# Cache projects, clients, tags, tasks, favorites and about two weeks of history in Room

## Problem

With no route to the server, users cannot read or edit their recent data, because the phone caches nothing. The app has no sync engine. The server is the source of truth, and the phone keeps what it last loaded. The PRD asks for projects, clients, tags, tasks, favorites and about two weeks of history, all readable offline, in a Room database.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:61
- docs/PRD.md:167
- docs/PRD.md:188
- docs/PRD.md:201
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- Build a Room database with entities for time entries, projects, clients, tags, tasks, favorites and account settings.
- Repositories read from Room and refresh from the server. A refresh replaces cached rows with the server's values.
- Keep about 14 days of history, counted in the account timezone from TKA-0023, and prune older rows.
- Keep archived projects and tags that cached entries still reference.
- Export the Room schema and track it in git for migrations.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] After one refresh, an instrumented test in airplane mode reads time entries, projects, clients, tags, tasks, favorites and account settings.
- [ ] History older than about 14 days, counted in the account timezone, is pruned.
- [ ] A refresh replaces stale rows with server values.
- [ ] Archived projects and tags that cached entries reference stay in the cache.
- [ ] The Room schema is exported and tracked.

resolution: "The settings refresh fetches /me/preferences once and hands the answer to onRefresh; the cache reads the favorites from it. MePreferences holds the raw JSON object and each reader decodes only its own key. Test ServerReaderTest.aTokklRefreshAsksForThePreferencesOnce failed before (two requests) and passes after. assembleDebug check: 208 JVM tests, lint 0 errors. No criteria waived."
---

# Send one GET /me/preferences per refresh on Tokkl

## Problem

Since TKA-0130, the account settings refresh asks for `GET /api/v9/me/preferences` to read `duration_format`. On a Tokkl server the cache refresh then asks for the same preferences again to read the favorites. Each foreground or sign-in therefore sends two identical requests. The refresh was built so that one `/me` answer serves the settings, the capabilities and the cache, and the preferences should work the same way. Toggl Track sends one request, because the cache does not read favorites there.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/AccountSettingsSource.kt:85 and :104-105 request `/me/preferences` for the duration format on every refresh (branch sift/w6-j3, commit 6251f17).
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/cache/ServerReader.kt:37 and :56 request `/me/preferences` again for the favorites when the server has them.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/TokklApplication.kt:51 wires `onRefresh` to `cache.refresh(api, me, ...)`, which passes only `/me` down. app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/cache/AccountCache.kt:38-39 calls `readServerSnapshot` with no preferences.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/AccountSettingsSource.kt:26-29 documents that one `/me` serves the settings, the capabilities and the cache.

## Direction

- Have `AccountSettingsSource` hand the preferences answer it already read to `onRefresh`, next to `/me`. Pass it through `AccountCache.refresh` to `readServerSnapshot`, which takes the favorites from it instead of sending its own request.
- Keep today's failure rules. Failed preferences keep the stored duration format and leave the favorites list null, so the cache keeps its rows. A server without the favorites capability still caches no favorites.
- Decode the response once, as one model that holds both `duration_format` and `favorites`. The duration format stays a loose JSON element, so a value that is not a string reads as improved (`MePreferences` in session/AccountSettings.kt).
- The KDoc of `TokklApplication.accountSettings` (TokklApplication.kt:38-42) describes the shared `/me` and says the settings file holds three values. Update it to name the shared preferences and the fourth value, `duration_format`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A refresh against a Tokkl server sends exactly one `GET /api/v9/me/preferences`, checked with a MockWebServer request count.
- [ ] The duration format and the cached favorites both come from that one answer.
- [ ] Failed preferences keep the stored duration format and the cached favorites.

# Build the reports screen and summary report, with an offline message instead of stale numbers

## Problem

Users cannot check this week's or month's totals on the phone. The billable share, the amounts, the daily chart and the project breakdown are out of reach too. All of these come from the server's Reports API, so with no connection the app has nothing current to show. The PRD rules out filling that gap with old numbers, so an offline reports screen has to say why it is empty.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:43 lists checking this week's or month's totals as a reason to open the app.
- docs/PRD.md:50 says Tokkl implements a subset of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`.
- docs/PRD.md:145 defines R1, a summary report with totals, billable share, amounts, a daily chart and a project breakdown for any date range.
- docs/PRD.md:149 defines R5. Reports need the server, and with no connection the screen says so instead of showing stale numbers.
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

Add a reports screen with a date-range picker. Range boundaries use the account timezone and week start from TKA-0023, so "this week" and "this month" cover the same hours as in the web app. For the chosen range the screen calls the Reports API v3 summary endpoints that Tokkl implements and shows:

- the total time and the billable share
- the amounts
- a daily bar chart
- a breakdown by project

The screen also hosts tabs for the detailed report, which TKA-0034 builds, and the weekly report, which TKA-0035 builds.

Report data is never cached on the device. When the phone is offline or the server is unreachable, the screen shows a clear message that reports need the server, and no numbers. Requests go to the configured server when the screen opens or the range changes, never on a timer.

The chart uses theme colours and carries a text label for each value, so colour is never the only signal. It has a TalkBack description that reads out the daily totals. All text lives in English string resources, every control has a TalkBack label, and the screen stays usable at the largest font scale.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Against a real Tokkl server, the totals, billable share, amounts, daily values and project breakdown match the web app's summary for the same range.
- [ ] With the network off or the server unreachable, the screen shows a message that reports need the server and shows no numbers.
- [ ] No report data is stored on the device.
- [ ] Each bar in the chart has a text label, and TalkBack reads a description of the chart.
- [ ] Range boundaries follow the account timezone and week start, checked with an account whose timezone differs from the device's.
- [ ] All text is in English string resources, every control has a TalkBack label, and the screen works at the largest font scale.

PinningTrustManager runs OkHttp's host check only after CA and pin refuse, so it never accepts; SSLPeerUnverifiedException maps to the same error. Before: prompt offered, pin stored, then Unreachable; after: host message, no prompt, no pin. CertificateHostMismatchTest covers unpinned and pinned; NoTrustAllTest still flags every HostnameVerifier except OkHostnameVerifier in PinningTrustManager.kt. Waived: the signed-in notice test (deferred to the wave 6 gate)."
---

# Explain a certificate host name mismatch instead of saying the server is unreachable

## Problem

TKA-0021 keeps OkHttp's host name check on, so a pinned self-signed certificate still has to name the host the user typed. A certificate whose subjectAltName lists another host or IP, or none at all, passes the pin and then fails that check with `SSLPeerUnverifiedException`. Sign-in maps the failure to `Unreachable`, so right after trusting the certificate the user reads "Can't reach the server. Check the URL and your connection." A plain `openssl req -x509` without `-addext subjectAltName=...` makes exactly such a certificate, so many self-signed setups will hit this. Nothing in the message points at the certificate.

## Expected behaviour

When the certificate does not name the server's host, sign-in says that the certificate does not cover that host and that it needs a subjectAltName with the host name or IP address. Nothing is sent, and host name verification stays on.

## Steps to reproduce

1. Serve Tokkl over https with a self-signed certificate whose subjectAltName does not list the host in the server URL, or has no subjectAltName.
2. Sign in with that URL and trust the certificate in the prompt.
3. Sign-in shows "Can't reach the server. Check the URL and your connection."

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignIn.kt:144 maps an `ApiError.Network` without an `UntrustedCertificateException` among its causes to `SignInResult.Unreachable` (:146).
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/TogglApiClient.kt:69 turns every `IOException`, `SSLPeerUnverifiedException` included, into `ApiError.Network`.
- app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml:44 holds the message the user sees.
- OkHttp 5.5.0 throws `SSLPeerUnverifiedException` ("Hostname ... not verified") from `okhttp3.internal.connection.ConnectPlan` after the handshake. Checked with `strings` on that class in the cached okhttp-android 5.5.0 AAR.
- Line numbers are from commit 74a8bc3 on branch sift/w5-s2.

## Direction

- Since TKA-0125, signed-in requests refused for trust reach `SessionTrustModel` (app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/trust/); a host name mismatch while signed in still stays quiet. Give the mismatch its own message on both paths, at sign-in and from a stored session, without offering to pin a certificate whose name does not match.
- Give an `SSLPeerUnverifiedException` from the shared client its own sign-in result and a message in string resources that names the host.
- Say how to fix it: reissue the certificate with the host name or IP address in its subjectAltName, or use the host it names.
- When the prompt can tell beforehand that the leaf does not cover the host, say so in the prompt instead of offering to trust a certificate that cannot work.
- Keep host name verification on. Never relax it for a pinned certificate.
- Follow the string resource, TalkBack, contrast, colour and font scale rules of the other sign-in messages.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A test with a certificate that does not name the host shows the host name message, not the unreachable one.
- [ ] The message is in string resources and names the host.
- [ ] No code path accepts a host that the certificate does not name.

# Detect server capabilities from /me fields and 404s on Tokkl-only endpoints

## Problem

The app has no minimum server version. Older Tokkl servers can lack the trash, undo of a delete, favorites or time resolution, and Toggl Track lacks all four once the app points at its base URL. Without detection those features break instead of hiding. The PRD says to check what the server can do and never compare versions. A missing key cannot be the favorites signal, because Tokkl leaves `favorites` out of `/me/preferences` until the user saves one. It also keeps any preference key a client sends.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:52
- docs/PRD.md:53
- docs/PRD.md:60
- docs/PRD.md:236
- docs/PRD.md:253
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/models/users.ts:28
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/models/users.ts:193
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/app.ts:174
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- Add a `Capabilities` model with flags for trash, restore, favorites and time resolution. Three signals set them.
- Time resolution is present when `GET /api/v9/me` carries `time_resolution_minutes`.
- Favorites are present when `GET /api/v9/me` carries Tokkl's `role` field, which marks a Tokkl server. On a Tokkl server, a missing `favorites` key in `/me/preferences` means an empty list, not missing support. Without `role`, as on Toggl, hide favorites.
- Trash and restore share one probe, `GET /api/app/trash`. A 404 hides both the trash and undo of a delete, because both live in Tokkl's app router under `/api/app`. Never probe the restore endpoint with a made-up entry ID.
- Persist the flags and refresh them with `/me`.
- Features read this model and never compare server versions.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A JVM test shows time resolution is false when `/me` lacks `time_resolution_minutes`.
- [ ] A JVM test shows favorites is false when `/me` lacks `role`.
- [ ] Favorites is true on a fresh Tokkl account with no saved favorites, shown by a JVM test and by an instrumented test against the real server.
- [ ] A JVM test shows trash and restore are both false when `GET /api/app/trash` returns 404.
- [ ] An instrumented test shows all flags are true against the current real Tokkl.
- [ ] The flags persist across restarts and refresh with `/me`.
- [ ] No code compares server versions, and no probe calls the restore endpoint.

# Port Tokkl's formatDuration and follow the account's duration format

## Problem

The timer, history and summary report show durations that must read exactly as in the web app (TKA-0039:35, TKA-0029:33, TKA-0026:49). The web app formats them with formatDuration (duration.ts:48-70) and the user's duration_format preference, which it reads from GET /me/preferences with 'improved' as the default (settings.ts:27, hooks.ts:35, settings.ts:93-94). TKA-0012 ported only the parsers and waived the format cases (TKA-0012:15). The PRD's port list names parseDuration, parseTimeOfDay and snapSpan, not formatDuration (docs/PRD.md:201). The app has neither formatDuration nor duration_format, and no open ticket asks for the formatting port.

## Evidence

- ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:48-70 defines formatDuration with the improved, classic and decimal formats, defaulting to improved.
- ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts:27-35 holds the formatDuration cases.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/settings.ts:27 reads `durationFormat` from the preferences' `duration_format`, defaulting to `'improved'`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/api/hooks.ts:35 fetches the preferences from `/me/preferences`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/settings.ts:93-94 formats every duration with formatDuration and the user's format.
- .ai/sift/archive/tracking/feature/TKA-0012--time-parsing-port.md:15 ports the parsers and waives the formatDuration cases.
- docs/PRD.md:201 lists the ports of parseDuration, parseTimeOfDay and snapSpan.
- .ai/sift/open/tracking/feature/TKA-0039--timer.md:35 ticks the elapsed time on the timer.
- .ai/sift/open/tracking/feature/TKA-0029--history.md:33 shows a total for each day in the history.
- .ai/sift/open/projects-and-reports/feature/TKA-0026--summary-report.md:49 requires the summary's totals to match the web app.
- absent: formatDuration and duration_format in app/src/main

## Direction

- Port formatDuration only, from ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:48-70, which stays read only. Put it in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time` beside the parseDuration port, with JavaScript semantics, as the kenkeep practice practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics describes.
- Mind the JavaScript traps. `toFixed(2)` rounds the double's exact value: in Node, 3618 seconds formats as `1.00 h`, while Java's `String.format("%.2f")` gives `1.01`. The decimal point never follows the locale. A format other than classic or decimal falls through to improved (duration.ts:60). Pin traps like these in DurationJsSemanticsTest with expected values from Node, as the practice says.
- Replay every case in duration.test.ts's format block (:27-35) verbatim in a JVM test whose header names the source file and the tokkl commit, as DurationTest does for the parser.
- Read duration_format from GET /me/preferences, as the web app does (settings.ts:27, hooks.ts:35), and use 'improved' when it is absent. The app already requests /me/preferences for the favorites (app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/cache/ServerReader.kt:56) into `ServerPreferences` (app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/TogglModels.kt:67-70). Persist the value with the account settings (app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/AccountSettings.kt:23) or the capabilities, whichever the existing code's patterns suggest.
- Expose a formatter that screens use, which applies the account's duration_format the way the web app's `duration()` helper does (settings.ts:93-94). The timer (TKA-0039), the history (TKA-0029) and the summary report (TKA-0026) then show the same text as the web app.
- Add one bullet to docs/PRD.md section 6.1, beside the parser ports at docs/PRD.md:201, naming the formatDuration port and its test file.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every formatDuration case from duration.test.ts passes in a JVM test that names its source file and tokkl commit.
- [ ] duration_format is read from GET /me/preferences and persisted, defaulting to 'improved' when absent.
- [ ] A formatter that follows duration_format is available to screens.
- [ ] docs/PRD.md section 6.1 names the format port.

```sh
  | sudo -n /usr/local/libexec/slabtop-notify remind
```

- The first stdin line is the title and everything after it is the body. Titles are limited to 200 characters and the whole message to 64 KiB. The command accepts at most 10 reminders per hour, because reminders and server alerts share the SMTP2GO quota.
[credential-related line omitted]

## Text to speech (Kokoro)

Installed and verified on 2026-10-12. `kokoro-tts` is on `PATH` for every project and runs Kokoro-82M locally on the CPU, with no API key.

```sh
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.mp3" --format mp3            # file in, mp3 out
echo 'Build finished.' | kokoro-tts - "$tmp/out.wav"          # stdin in
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.wav" --voice bm_george --speed 1.1
kokoro-tts --help-voices                                      # 54 voices; af_heart is the default
```

- `~/.local/bin/kokoro-tts` is a wrapper around the upstream `kokoro-tts` CLI ([nazdridoy/kokoro-tts](https://github.com/nazdridoy/kokoro-tts), 2.3.2) in the venv `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv`. It adds `--model` and `--voices` pointing at the v1.0 files in `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/`, and `--voice af_heart` (or `$KOKORO_TTS_VOICE`), because the upstream default is an interactive voice picker that blocks an agent. Flags you pass explicitly win.
- Other flags: `--lang` (default `en-us`; see `--help-languages`), voice blends such as `--voice "af_sarah:60,am_adam:40"`, `.epub`/`.pdf` input, `--split-output <dir>`. Output is `wav` or `mp3`; use `ffmpeg` for anything else.
- Speed is roughly 170 words per minute at `--speed 1.0`. Rendering takes about a third of the audio's length on this machine, so a five minute clip takes under two minutes.
- Write scripts and audio to a temporary directory (`tmp=$(mktemp -d)`), never to the home directory, unless the user names a destination.
- To upgrade: `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv/bin/python -m pip install -U kokoro-tts`. The venv has no system `ensurepip`; pip was bootstrapped into it.

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics.md
---
type: practice
title: Port Tokkl's TypeScript with JavaScript semantics and verbatim test cases
description: >-
  The Kotlin time parsers copy JavaScript's whitespace, rounding and replace
  rules, return null for NaN, and reuse the .test.ts cases verbatim.
tags:
  - time
  - parsing
  - testing
  - tokkl
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The ports in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time` (`Duration.kt` for `parseDuration`, `TimeOfDay.kt` for `parseTimeOfDay`, `Snap.kt` for `snapSpan`, and `TimeZone.kt`, which accepts the timezone names Intl accepts) behave like the TypeScript they port, including where Kotlin differs from JavaScript. `JsSemantics.kt` has the helpers. JavaScript's `\s` and `String.prototype.trim` use the ECMAScript WhiteSpace and LineTerminator characters, while Java's `\s` matches only ASCII and Kotlin's `trim()` uses yet another set. `Math.round` rounds halves up and `kotlin.math.round` rounds them to even. JavaScript's `replace(',', '.')` changes only the first comma, so the port calls `replaceFirst`.

A Long cannot hold NaN, Infinity or integers from 2^63 up, so where the TypeScript returns one of those the port returns null. `parseDuration` returns NaN for the unit `constructor`, because its unit lookup finds that key on `Object.prototype`, and Infinity once a number of 300-odd digits overflows a double.

`DurationTest` and `TimeOfDayTest` hold the cases of the matching `.test.ts` file verbatim, and their header names the tokkl commit they were copied at. To follow an upstream change, copy the cases again and update that commit. `DurationJsSemanticsTest` pins the JavaScript traps in its own file, since they are not in `duration.test.ts` and a later diff against tokkl leaves them alone. Its expected values come from running the TypeScript file in Node 24, where `node file.mts` strips the types.

Where the TypeScript throws a RangeError, `snapSpan` throws `IllegalArgumentException`. Within hours of the end of a JavaScript Date's range, near the year 275760, the TypeScript can also return an invalid Date. An `Instant` has no such value, so the port throws there too, and `SnapJsDateRangeTest` pins that edge.

`parseTimeZone` was checked against Intl in Node 24.21 (ICU 78.3, tz 2026c), but its results still depend on the device. A name resolves only if the device's tz database lists it or `INTL_ONLY_NAMES` maps it, and that database can be older than the server's. Under tz 2026c America/Edmonton stays at -06:00 all year, while the JDK 21 on this machine ships 2026b and still gives -07:00 in winter.

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/map-tokkl-toggl-api.md
---
type: map
title: 'Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions'
description: >-
  Tokkl speaks a subset of Toggl API v9 at /api/v9 and Reports v3 at
  /reports/api/v3 with Basic auth, plus Tokkl-only fields and endpoints.
tags:
  - api
  - toggl
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-tokkl-toggl-api
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Tokkl implements a subset of the Toggl Track API v9 at `https://<server>/api/v9` and of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`. A client that accepts a base URL needs no other change to talk to it. The server URL may carry a path, such as `https://example.com/tokkl`. Toggl Track's API lives at `api.track.toggl.com`.

[credential-related line omitted]

Tokkl adds `role` and `time_resolution_minutes` to `GET /api/v9/me`, and the server snaps stopped entries to that resolution. Tokkl-only endpoints outside the Toggl API cover the trash (`GET/DELETE /api/app/trash`, `DELETE /api/app/trash/{id}`) and undoing a delete (`POST /api/app/time_entries/{id}/restore`). Favorites are a Tokkl key in `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

The first version targets Tokkl only. Toggl Track comes later by pointing the app at Toggl's base URL, and that version hides the Tokkl-only features.

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/map-account-settings-source-and-account-time.md
---
type: map
title: 'Account settings: AccountSettingsSource, AccountTime and NoDeviceZoneTest'
description: >-
  Sign-in stores timezone, week start and resolution from /me; onStart
  refreshes them. AccountTime gives zone and dates, never the device's.
tags:
  - time
  - api
  - android
  - settings
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-account-settings-source-and-account-time
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
  - map-signin-session-settings-and-navigation-host
  - map-connection-trust-store-pinning-and-prompts
  - practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
`session/AccountSettings` holds the `GET /api/v9/me` fields that decide how the app shows and reads times: `timezone`, `beginning_of_week` and Tokkl's optional `time_resolution_minutes`. It holds no credential. Sign-in stores them from its own `/me`. A `/me` without `timezone` or `beginning_of_week`, or with a week start outside 0 to 6, fails sign-in as `SignInResult.NotTokkl`. Toggl Track and older Tokkl servers leave out `time_resolution_minutes` and do not snap, so it stays null for them.

`session/AccountSettingsSource` is the one place screens read the settings from. `TokklApplication.accountSettings` builds it over `AccountSettingsStore`, which keeps them in the private preferences file `account_settings`, so screens have them offline and after a restart. `MainActivity.onStart` calls `refresh()`, so a change made in the web app reaches the phone. A second `refresh()` while one runs gets the running job instead of sending another request. A failed request or write keeps what is stored, and a refresh drops its answer when sign-in stored newer settings while the request ran.

`AccountSettings.time()` gives a `time/AccountTime`: the account's zone, a clock in that zone, `now()`, `today()`, `local(instant)`, `startOfWeek(date)` and `snapUnsent(start, stop)`. It returns null when `parseTimeZone` cannot resolve the name, as when the server's Intl knows a zone that the phone's tz database lacks. There is no fallback to the device's zone or to UTC. A screen without an `AccountTime` must show no clock times or dates, take no time input and name the zone it does not know. Durations, the running clock, Start and Stop need no zone and keep working.

`NoDeviceZoneTest`, a JVM test, reads the constant pool of every compiled app class through `ClassReferences` and fails on `ZoneId.systemDefault`, `TimeZone.getDefault` (java.util and ICU), `Clock.systemDefaultZone`, a `now()` without arguments on the java.time date and time types, and a `Calendar` or `GregorianCalendar` built without a zone. Its `ALLOWED` set is empty, and a class added there needs a comment saying why the account's zone will not do. Take the zone, the clock and the dates from `AccountTime` instead.

.ai/kenkeep/nodes/map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors.md
---
type: map
title: 'API client: one OkHttpClient, ServerUrl, TogglApiClient and ApiError'
description: >-
  The api package holds the app's only OkHttpClient, base URL handling, JSON
  calls, sendNoContent for empty bodies and typed errors.
tags:
  - api
  - network
  - okhttp
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - map-tokkl-toggl-api
  - practice-talk-only-to-configured-server
  - practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http
  - map-connection-trust-store-pinning-and-prompts
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The HTTP code lives in `app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/`. `SharedHttpClient.instance` is the app's only `OkHttpClient`, so its connection pool, dispatcher and security settings apply to every request. New security settings, such as the trust manager for a pinned self-signed certificate, go into its `build()`, never into a second client. `SingleHttpClientTest` reads the constant pool of every compiled app class through `ClassReferences`, the class-file reader in `app/src/test` that `NoDeviceZoneTest` shares, and fails when any class but `SharedHttpClient` builds an HTTP client, so an import alias or a fully qualified name cannot hide one.

`ConfiguredServerGuard`, an interceptor on that client, throws `HostNotAllowedException` for any request whose scheme, host or port differs from `ConfiguredServer.current`. It does so before OkHttp connects, so no credentials leave the phone, and `TogglApiClient` returns it as `ApiError.HostNotAllowed(origin)` with the origin as `scheme://host:port`. `current` is the stored server, or the URL being tried inside `ConfiguredServer.signingIn`. With neither, every request is refused. The guard also refuses a plain `http://` server the user has not confirmed, and the trust manager refuses a self-signed certificate the user has not pinned (see the connection trust node). OkHttp's own redirects are off (`followRedirects(false)`, `followSslRedirects(false)`). The guard follows a redirect itself, and only to the same origin.

[credential-related line omitted]

`TogglApiClient.get` and `send` decode a 2xx body with a kotlinx.serialization deserializer, and an empty body fails to decode. Calls that expect no content use `sendNoContent`, which treats any 2xx as success and ignores the body. Tokkl's DELETE routes for time entries, tasks, clients and tags answer 200 with an empty body, while the project DELETE answers 200 with the project id as JSON (`../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts`). `sendNoContent` handles both.

Every call returns `ApiResult.Success` or `ApiResult.Failure(ApiError)`. 403 maps to `AuthFailed`, 429 to `RateLimited` with the `Retry-After` seconds (null when the header is missing or not a whole number), and any other status outside 2xx to `UnexpectedStatus`. `Network` wraps the `IOException` of a request that got no response. `InvalidResponse` is a 2xx body that is not the expected JSON. `TrustRefused` covers `CleartextNotConfirmed` and `UntrustedCertificate`, which the client also reports to its `TrustListener`.

Agents get `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-headless` and `DISPLAY=:0` from `t3.service`. Plasma, sddm and KRdp were removed from the boot path on 2026-10-02 (default target is `multi-user.target`). Setup and rollback are in `~/ai-env/headless-desktop.md`.

## Sending a reminder email

Agents can email the user a reminder (for example, a follow-up the user wants to deal with later) through the same encrypted pipeline:

```sh
{ echo 'kenkeep: stop tests leaking FOLDER_SUMMARIES into /tmp'; cat notes.md; } \
  | sudo -n /usr/local/libexec/slabtop-notify remind
```

- The first stdin line is the title and everything after it is the body. Titles are limited to 200 characters and the whole message to 64 KiB. The command accepts at most 10 reminders per hour, because reminders and server alerts share the SMTP2GO quota.
[credential-related line omitted]
- Never write a wrapper script and ask the user to run it with sudo. A file in the home directory can be changed by any process running as e0ipso, so running it as root hands root to that process. Pipe into the command above instead.
- The email subject is always `[punta-des-patro]: Server notification`. The title appears only inside the encrypted body, as `Reminder: <title>`.
- Installed and verified on 2026-10-02: a test reminder was delivered, and `sudo -n` refused other subcommands and extra arguments. The staging directory `~/.local/state/slabtop-remind` and the old `~/ai-env/reminders/send-reminder.sh` wrapper were removed.

## Text to speech (Kokoro)

Installed and verified on 2026-10-12. `kokoro-tts` is on `PATH` for every project and runs Kokoro-82M locally on the CPU, with no API key.

```sh
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.mp3" --format mp3            # file in, mp3 out
echo 'Build finished.' | kokoro-tts - "$tmp/out.wav"          # stdin in
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.wav" --voice bm_george --speed 1.1
kokoro-tts --help-voices                                      # 54 voices; af_heart is the default
```

- `~/.local/bin/kokoro-tts` is a wrapper around the upstream `kokoro-tts` CLI ([nazdridoy/kokoro-tts](https://github.com/nazdridoy/kokoro-tts), 2.3.2) in the venv `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv`. It adds `--model` and `--voices` pointing at the v1.0 files in `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/`, and `--voice af_heart` (or `$KOKORO_TTS_VOICE`), because the upstream default is an interactive voice picker that blocks an agent. Flags you pass explicitly win.
- Other flags: `--lang` (default `en-us`; see `--help-languages`), voice blends such as `--voice "af_sarah:60,am_adam:40"`, `.epub`/`.pdf` input, `--split-output <dir>`. Output is `wav` or `mp3`; use `ffmpeg` for anything else.
- Speed is roughly 170 words per minute at `--speed 1.0`. Rendering takes about a third of the audio's length on this machine, so a five minute clip takes under two minutes.
- Write scripts and audio to a temporary directory (`tmp=$(mktemp -d)`), never to the home directory, unless the user names a destination.
- To upgrade: `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv/bin/python -m pip install -U kokoro-tts`. The venv has no system `ensurepip`; pip was bootstrapped into it.
