app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/TokklTestServer.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl

import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
import java.util.Properties

/**
 * The real Tokkl server that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh starts for the
 * instrumented tests, and the account it creates there. The script passes the
[credential-related line omitted]
 * token are in a file on the device, which only the shell user can read; as
 * instrumentation arguments they would show in the arguments of the adb
 * process that starts the tests. No instrumented test replaces Tokkl with a
 * mock or a fake server.
 */
object TokklTestServer {

    /** The server's base URL as the emulator reaches it, such as `http://10.0.2.2:32768`. */
    val url: ServerUrl by lazy {
        val url = argument("tokklBaseUrl")
        ServerUrl.parse(url) ?: error("tokklBaseUrl is not an http or https URL: $url")
    }

    /**
     * The same server behind a TLS proxy with a self-signed certificate that
     * the script makes for each run, such as `https://10.0.2.2:32769`. The
     * certificate names 10.0.2.2 as an IP address, so the host name check
     * passes once the certificate is pinned.
     */
    val tlsUrl: ServerUrl by lazy {
        val url = argument("tokklTlsBaseUrl")
        ServerUrl.parse(url) ?: error("tokklTlsBaseUrl is not an https URL: $url")
    }

    /** The proxy certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, as `openssl x509 -noout -fingerprint -sha256` prints it. */
    val tlsFingerprint: String by lazy { argument("tokklTlsFingerprint") }

    val email: String by lazy { argument("tokklEmail") }

[credential-related line omitted]

[credential-related line omitted]

    private val credentials: Properties by lazy {
        val path = argument("tokklCredentialsFile")
        Properties().apply { load(DeviceShell.run("cat $path").reader()) }
    }

    private fun credential(name: String): String =
        credentials.getProperty(name)
            ?: error(
                "$name is missing from the file in tokklCredentialsFile. " +
                    "Run the instrumented tests with scripts/instrumented-tests.sh, which writes it.",
            )

    private fun argument(name: String): String =
        InstrumentationRegistry.getArguments().getString(name)
            ?: error(
                "The instrumentation argument $name is missing. " +
                    "Run the instrumented tests with scripts/instrumented-tests.sh, which starts the server.",
            )
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/trust/ChangedCertificateWhileSignedInTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.trust

import android.content.Context
import androidx.annotation.StringRes
import androidx.compose.ui.test.SemanticsMatcher
import androidx.compose.ui.test.assertIsDisplayed
import androidx.compose.ui.test.hasClickAction
import androidx.compose.ui.test.hasText
import androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createAndroidComposeRule
import androidx.compose.ui.test.performClick
import androidx.compose.ui.test.performScrollTo
import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MainActivity
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.R
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SignedOut
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerCertificate
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.awaitText
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerTrustStore
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Session
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonPrimitive
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Rule
import org.junit.Test
import org.junit.rules.ExternalResource
import org.junit.rules.RuleChain

/**
 * A session signed in to the Tokkl container behind the harness's TLS proxy,
 * whose stored pin belongs to an earlier certificate: what the app sees after
 * the server's key was regenerated.
 */
@SignedOut
class ChangedCertificateWhileSignedInTest {

    private val context: Context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
    private val endpoint = TokklTestServer.tlsUrl.api("me")
    private val composeRule = createAndroidComposeRule<MainActivity>()

    @get:Rule
    val rules: RuleChain = RuleChain.outerRule(SessionRule())
        .around(object : ExternalResource() {
            override fun before() {
                ServerTrustStore(context).pinCertificate(ServerCertificate(endpoint.host, endpoint.port, OLD_SHA256, subject = ""))
[credential-related line omitted]
            }
        })
        .around(composeRule)

    private val newSha256 = TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint.replace(":", "").lowercase()

    @Test
    fun changedCertificateShowsThePromptOnTheHomeScreenAndConnectsOnlyOnceTrusted() {
        composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.launch_message))

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_changed_title)), "the certificate prompt shows")
        composeRule.onNode(hasText(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint)).performScrollTo().assertIsDisplayed()
        assertTrue("requests are refused before the user trusts it", me() is ApiResult.Failure)

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_trust)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()

        awaitGone(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_changed_title)), "the prompt closes")
        assertEquals(newSha256, ServerTrustStore(context).pinnedSha256(endpoint.host, endpoint.port))
        val body = (me() as? ApiResult.Success)?.value ?: throw AssertionError("GET /api/v9/me still fails after the user trusted the certificate")
        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.email, body.getValue("email").jsonPrimitive.content)
    }

    @Test
    fun cancellingKeepsRequestsRefusedAndTheNoticeReopensThePrompt() {
        composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.launch_message))
        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_changed_title)), "the certificate prompt shows")

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_warning_cancel)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.trust_refused_certificate)), "the notice shows")
        assertEquals(
            ApiError.UntrustedCertificate(ServerCertificate(endpoint.host, endpoint.port, newSha256, SUBJECT), changed = true),
            (me() as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error,
        )
        assertEquals(OLD_SHA256, ServerTrustStore(context).pinnedSha256(endpoint.host, endpoint.port))

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.trust_refused_review)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_certificate_changed_title)), "the prompt opens again")
    }

    /** `GET /api/v9/me` with the stored session's client, as the app sends it. */
    private fun me(): ApiResult<JsonObject> {
        val client = Session(context).client() ?: throw AssertionError("no stored session")
        return client.get(endpoint, JsonObject.serializer())
    }

    private fun awaitGone(matcher: SemanticsMatcher, what: String) {
        composeRule.waitUntil(what, NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS) { composeRule.onAllNodes(matcher).fetchSemanticsNodes().isEmpty() }
    }

    private fun awaitNode(matcher: SemanticsMatcher, what: String) {
        composeRule.waitUntil(what, NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS) { composeRule.onAllNodes(matcher).fetchSemanticsNodes().isNotEmpty() }
    }

    private fun string(@StringRes id: Int): String = context.getString(id)

    private companion object {
        const val NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000L

        /** The fingerprint of the certificate the user trusted before the server's key changed. */
        val OLD_SHA256 = "00".repeat(32)

        /** The subject of the certificate that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh makes. */
        const val SUBJECT = "CN=Tokkl instrumented tests"
    }
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/trust/UnconfirmedHttpWhileSignedInTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.trust

import android.content.Context
import androidx.annotation.StringRes
import androidx.compose.ui.test.SemanticsMatcher
import androidx.compose.ui.test.assertIsDisplayed
import androidx.compose.ui.test.assertIsEnabled
import androidx.compose.ui.test.assertIsNotEnabled
import androidx.compose.ui.test.hasClickAction
import androidx.compose.ui.test.hasText
import androidx.compose.ui.test.isToggleable
import androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createAndroidComposeRule
import androidx.compose.ui.test.performClick
import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.DeviceShell
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MainActivity
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.R
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SignedOut
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.awaitText
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerTrustStore
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Session
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Rule
import org.junit.Test
import org.junit.rules.ExternalResource
import org.junit.rules.RuleChain

/**
 * A session stored for the plain http Tokkl container with no confirmation
 * recorded, as after an install from before the http warning existed.
 */
@SignedOut
class UnconfirmedHttpWhileSignedInTest {

    private val context: Context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
    private val composeRule = createAndroidComposeRule<MainActivity>()

    @get:Rule
    val rules: RuleChain = RuleChain.outerRule(SessionRule())
        .around(object : ExternalResource() {
            override fun before() {
[credential-related line omitted]
            }
        })
        .around(composeRule)

    @Test
    fun unconfirmedHttpSessionShowsTheWarningAndSendsNothingUntilConfirmed() {
        composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.launch_message))

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_title)), "the http warning shows")
        continueButton().assertIsNotEnabled()
        // Neither a tap outside the dialog nor Back closes it.
        DeviceShell.run("input tap 4 ${context.resources.displayMetrics.heightPixels / 2}")
        DeviceShell.run("input keyevent KEYCODE_BACK")
        composeRule.waitForIdle()
        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_title))).assertIsDisplayed()
        assertEquals(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(TokklTestServer.url), (me() as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error)

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_understood)) and isToggleable()).performClick()
        continueButton().assertIsEnabled().performClick()

        awaitGone(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_title)), "the warning closes")
        assertTrue("the confirmation is stored", ServerTrustStore(context).isCleartextConfirmed(TokklTestServer.url))
        assertTrue("requests reach the server", me() is ApiResult.Success)
    }

    @Test
    fun cancellingKeepsRequestsRefusedAndTheNoticeReopensTheWarning() {
        composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.launch_message))
        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_title)), "the http warning shows")

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_warning_cancel)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.trust_refused_http)), "the notice shows")
        assertEquals(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(TokklTestServer.url), (me() as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error)
        assertFalse(ServerTrustStore(context).isCleartextConfirmed(TokklTestServer.url))

        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.trust_refused_review)) and hasClickAction()).performClick()

        awaitNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_title)), "the warning opens again")
    }

    private fun continueButton() =
        composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(R.string.sign_in_http_warning_continue)) and hasClickAction())

    /** `GET /api/v9/me` with the stored session's client, as the app sends it. */
    private fun me(): ApiResult<JsonObject> {
        val client = Session(context).client() ?: throw AssertionError("no stored session")
        return client.get(client.server.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())
    }

    private fun awaitGone(matcher: SemanticsMatcher, what: String) {
        composeRule.waitUntil(what, NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS) { composeRule.onAllNodes(matcher).fetchSemanticsNodes().isEmpty() }
    }

    private fun awaitNode(matcher: SemanticsMatcher, what: String) {
        composeRule.waitUntil(what, NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS) { composeRule.onAllNodes(matcher).fetchSemanticsNodes().isNotEmpty() }
    }

    private fun string(@StringRes id: Int): String = context.getString(id)

    private companion object {
        const val NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000L
    }
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/AccountSettingsServerTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session

import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiCredentials
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
import java.time.DayOfWeek
import java.time.ZoneId
import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.buildJsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.put
import org.junit.After
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Rule
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The app's own settings source reads the account settings from the real Tokkl container. The
 * test changes the account's timezone and first day of the week, and puts back the UTC and
 * Monday that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh set up.
 */
class AccountSettingsServerTest {

    @get:Rule
    val session = SessionRule()

    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext

[credential-related line omitted]

    @After
    fun restoreTheAccount() {
        try {
            putMe(timezone = "UTC", beginningOfWeek = 1)
        } finally {
            AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
        }
    }

    @Test
    fun refreshReadsTheSettingsFromTheServerAndKeepsThem() {
        // Tokkl keeps the name as sent. Intl accepts it in any case, and so does parseTimeZone.
        putMe(timezone = "america/sao_paulo", beginningOfWeek = 0)
        val source = (context.applicationContext as TokklApplication).accountSettings

        runBlocking { source.refresh().join() }

        // Tokkl's instance time resolution is 5 minutes unless an admin changes it.
        val expected = AccountSettings("america/sao_paulo", DayOfWeek.SUNDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = 5.0)
        assertEquals(expected, source.state.value)
        assertEquals("the stored settings", expected, AccountSettingsStore(context).load())
        assertEquals(ZoneId.of("America/Sao_Paulo"), expected.time()?.zone)
    }

    private fun putMe(timezone: String, beginningOfWeek: Int) {
        val body = buildJsonObject {
            put("timezone", timezone)
            put("beginning_of_week", beginningOfWeek)
        }
        // Fails with the error alone, so neither a response body nor a secret lands in the message.
        val result = client.send("PUT", client.server.api("me"), body, JsonObject.serializer())
        if (result is ApiResult.Failure) {
            val error = result.error
            val reason = if (error is ApiError.Http) "HTTP ${error.status}" else error::class.simpleName
            throw AssertionError("PUT /api/v9/me failed: $reason")
        }
    }
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/AccountSettingsRestartTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session

import android.os.Process
import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
import java.io.File
import java.time.DayOfWeek
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNotEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.FixMethodOrder
import org.junit.Test
import org.junit.runners.MethodSorters

/**
 * The account settings survive a restart of the app process, so screens have them offline. The
 * steps run in name order, and the test orchestrator (app/build.gradle.kts) starts a new process
 * for each. Each step reads what the step before stored through the app's own settings source,
 * which sends no request. A step fails if it runs in the same process as the one before.
 */
@FixMethodOrder(MethodSorters.NAME_ASCENDING)
class AccountSettingsRestartTest {

    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext

    private val source get() = (context.applicationContext as TokklApplication).accountSettings

    /** Names the process that ran the step before. */
    private val previousStep = File(context.cacheDir, "AccountSettingsRestartTest.process")

    @Test
    fun step1StoresSettingsWithATimeResolution() {
        AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
        source.store(WITH_RESOLUTION)
        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
    }

    @Test
    fun step2KeepsThemAndStoresSettingsWithout() {
        assertNewProcess()
        assertEquals(WITH_RESOLUTION, source.state.value)
        source.store(WITHOUT_RESOLUTION)
        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
    }

    @Test
    fun step3KeepsTheSettingsWithoutATimeResolution() {
        try {
            assertNewProcess()
            assertEquals(WITHOUT_RESOLUTION, source.state.value)
        } finally {
            AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
            previousStep.delete()
        }
    }

    private fun assertNewProcess() {
        assertTrue("the step before did not run", previousStep.exists())
        assertNotEquals("the step before ran in this same process", previousStep.readText(), currentProcess())
    }

    /** A PID can be reused, so the process start time goes with it. */
    private fun currentProcess(): String = "${Process.myPid()}@${Process.getStartElapsedRealtime()}"

    private companion object {
        val WITH_RESOLUTION = AccountSettings("Europe/Madrid", DayOfWeek.SUNDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = 15.0)
        val WITHOUT_RESOLUTION = AccountSettings("Asia/Kolkata", DayOfWeek.SATURDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = null)
    }
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/PinnedCertificateTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerConfigStore
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerTrustStore
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonPrimitive
import org.junit.After
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Before
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The shared client against the real Tokkl container behind the TLS proxy
 * that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh starts with a self-signed certificate.
 * No system or user CA trusts that certificate, so only a pin lets a request
 * through, and Android's own TLS stack runs the handshake.
 */
class PinnedCertificateTest {

    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
    private val servers = ServerConfigStore(context)
    private val trust = ServerTrustStore(context)
    private val server = TokklTestServer.tlsUrl
    private val me = server.api("me")
[credential-related line omitted]

    @Before
    fun configureTheServer() {
        servers.save(server)
        trust.clear()
    }

    @After
    fun forgetTheServer() {
        trust.clear()
        servers.clear()
    }

    @Test
    fun unpinnedCertificateFailsTheHandshakeWithItsFingerprint() {
        val refusal = refusal(client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer()))

        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint, refusal.certificate.displaySha256)
        assertEquals(me.host to me.port, refusal.certificate.host to refusal.certificate.port)
        assertFalse(refusal.changed)
    }

    @Test
    fun pinnedCertificateReachesTokkl() {
        trust.pinCertificate(pin(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint.replace(":", "").lowercase()))

        val result = client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer())

        val body = (result as? ApiResult.Success)?.value ?: throw AssertionError("GET /api/v9/me failed: ${(result as ApiResult.Failure).error.javaClass.simpleName}")
        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.email, body.getValue("email").jsonPrimitive.content)
    }

    @Test
    fun anotherPinnedCertificateFailsAsAChangedCertificate() {
        trust.pinCertificate(pin("00".repeat(32)))

        val refusal = refusal(client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer()))

        assertTrue(refusal.changed)
        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint, refusal.certificate.displaySha256)
    }

    private fun pin(sha256: String) = ServerCertificate(me.host, me.port, sha256, subject = "")

    /** The pinning trust manager's refusal, which the client reports as its own error. */
    private fun refusal(result: ApiResult<*>): ApiError.UntrustedCertificate {
        val error = (result as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error
        return error as? ApiError.UntrustedCertificate ?: throw AssertionError("expected a refused certificate, got ${error ?: "a success"}")
    }
}

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/ClockAndTotalStylesFitTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme

import androidx.compose.foundation.background
import androidx.compose.foundation.layout.Box
import androidx.compose.foundation.layout.Column
import androidx.compose.foundation.layout.padding
import androidx.compose.foundation.layout.width
import androidx.compose.material3.MaterialTheme
import androidx.compose.material3.Text
import androidx.compose.runtime.Composable
import androidx.compose.runtime.getValue
import androidx.compose.runtime.mutableStateOf
import androidx.compose.runtime.setValue
import androidx.compose.ui.Modifier
import androidx.compose.ui.geometry.Rect
import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.Color
import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.toPixelMap
import androidx.compose.ui.platform.LocalDensity
import androidx.compose.ui.platform.testTag
import androidx.compose.ui.test.captureToImage
import androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createComposeRule
import androidx.compose.ui.test.onNodeWithTag
import androidx.compose.ui.text.TextLayoutResult
import androidx.compose.ui.text.TextStyle
import androidx.compose.ui.unit.dp
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Rule
import org.junit.Test
import org.junit.rules.RuleChain

/**
 * Checks the clock and totals styles at Android's largest font scale before any screen uses
 * them. The longest realistic clock and a multi-digit total sit one under the other in a narrow
 * column. Every glyph must show in full, and neither sample may overlap the other.
 *
 * The column is narrower than either sample, so both wrap. A style's line height matters only
 * between lines. Compose keeps the font's own ascent above the first line and its descent below
 * the last, so a single line keeps its glyphs whatever line height the style sets. Each line's
 * glyphs have to stay inside that line's box. A line height shorter than the glyphs fails,
 * because neighbouring lines then run into each other.
 *
 * Both samples share one layout. Each check draws one sample and leaves the other transparent,
 * so every inked pixel belongs to the sample under test.
 */
class ClockAndTotalStylesFitTest {
    private val composeRule = createComposeRule()

    /** The font scale is set before the test activity starts, so it never has to restart. */
    @get:Rule
    val rules: RuleChain = RuleChain.outerRule(SystemFontScale(SystemFontScale.LARGEST)).around(composeRule)

    private var inked by mutableStateOf(CLOCK)
    private val layouts = mutableMapOf<String, TextLayoutResult>()
    private var fontScale = 0f

    @Test
    fun clockAndTotalFitInANarrowColumn() {
        composeRule.setContent {
            fontScale = LocalDensity.current.fontScale
            TokklTheme {
                // The margin around the column shows glyphs that reach past their box.
                Box(Modifier.testTag(HOST).background(MaterialTheme.colorScheme.background).padding(MARGIN)) {
                    Column(Modifier.width(NARROW_WIDTH)) {
                        Sample(CLOCK, CLOCK_SAMPLE, TokklTheme.clockTextStyle)
                        Sample(TOTAL, TOTAL_SAMPLE, TokklTheme.totalTextStyle)
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        composeRule.waitForIdle()
        assertEquals("font scale", SystemFontScale.LARGEST, fontScale)

        for (sample in listOf(CLOCK, TOTAL)) {
            inked = sample
            composeRule.waitForIdle()
            val layout = layouts.getValue(sample)
            assertTrue("$sample fits on one line, so its line height goes untested", layout.lineCount > 1)
            assertFalse("$sample layout overflows its height", layout.didOverflowHeight)
            assertGlyphsInsideTheirLines(sample, bounds(sample), layout)
        }
        assertFalse("$CLOCK overlaps $TOTAL", bounds(CLOCK).overlaps(bounds(TOTAL)))
    }

    @Composable
    private fun Sample(tag: String, text: String, style: TextStyle) {
        Text(
            text = text,
            style = style,
            color = if (inked == tag) MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onBackground else Color.Transparent,
            onTextLayout = { layouts[tag] = it },
            modifier = Modifier.testTag(tag),
        )
    }

    /**
     * Every pixel that differs from the margin's colour lies inside [box]. The rows that hold
     * such pixels form one band per line, and each band lies inside its line's box. The samples
     * are digits and colons, so a gap of empty rows separates the glyphs of lines that keep
     * apart.
     */
    private fun assertGlyphsInsideTheirLines(sample: String, box: Rect, layout: TextLayoutResult) {
        val host = bounds(HOST)
        val pixels = composeRule.onNodeWithTag(HOST).captureToImage().toPixelMap()
        val background = pixels[0, 0]
        val outside = mutableListOf<Pair<Int, Int>>()
        val inkedRows = (0 until pixels.height).filter { y ->
            var inked = false
            for (x in 0 until pixels.width) {
                if (pixels[x, y] == background) continue
                inked = true
                // The centre of the pixel, in window coordinates like the boxes.
                if (!box.containsPoint(host.left + x + 0.5f, host.top + y + 0.5f)) outside += x to y
            }
            inked
        }
        assertTrue(
            "$sample has ${outside.size} glyph pixels outside its box $box, such as ${outside.take(5)} " +
                "in the host at $host",
            outside.isEmpty(),
        )

        val bands = inkedRows.bands().map { (first, last) -> host.top + first..host.top + last + 1f }
        val lines = (0 until layout.lineCount).map { box.top + layout.getLineTop(it)..box.top + layout.getLineBottom(it) }
        assertEquals("$sample glyph rows $bands for lines $lines", lines.size, bands.size)
        for ((i, band) in bands.withIndex()) {
            val line = lines[i]
            assertTrue(
                "$sample line $i glyphs at $band leave the line's box $line",
                band.start >= line.start && band.endInclusive <= line.endInclusive,
            )
        }
    }

    /** Runs of consecutive row numbers, as first and last row. */
    private fun List<Int>.bands(): List<Pair<Int, Int>> =
        fold(mutableListOf<Pair<Int, Int>>()) { runs, row ->
            val last = runs.lastOrNull()
            if (last != null && last.second == row - 1) runs[runs.lastIndex] = last.first to row else runs += row to row
            runs
        }

    private fun bounds(tag: String): Rect =
        composeRule.onNodeWithTag(tag, useUnmergedTree = true).fetchSemanticsNode().boundsInWindow

    private fun Rect.containsPoint(x: Float, y: Float): Boolean = x in left..right && y in top..bottom

    private companion object {
        const val HOST = "host"
        const val CLOCK = "clock"
        const val TOTAL = "total"

        /** The running clock just before it turns a day. */
        const val CLOCK_SAMPLE = "23:59:59"

        /** A report total over several years, in Tokkl's h:mm:ss format (packages/common/duration.ts). */
        const val TOTAL_SAMPLE = "9999:59:59"

        /** Narrower than either sample at the largest font scale, so both wrap. */
        val NARROW_WIDTH = 100.dp
        val MARGIN = 24.dp
    }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/time/AccountTime.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time

import java.time.Clock
import java.time.DayOfWeek
import java.time.Instant
import java.time.LocalDate
import java.time.ZoneId
import java.time.ZonedDateTime
import java.time.temporal.TemporalAdjusters

/**
 * Times in the account's timezone, the one zone the app shows and reads times in (docs/PRD.md
 * S7). Screens take the zone, the current time, today's date and week boundaries from here and
 * never from the device: no `ZoneId.systemDefault()`, no `TimeZone.getDefault()` and no `now()`
 * without a clock. [com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.AccountSettings.time] builds one.
 *
 * Manual entry (TKA-0040) adds the conversion from a typed wall-clock time to an instant here,
 * as a port of zonedToUtc in ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts.
 *
 * @param clock where the current instant comes from. Its own zone does not matter.
 */
class AccountTime(
    val zone: ZoneId,
    val firstDayOfWeek: DayOfWeek,
    private val timeResolutionMinutes: Double?,
    clock: Clock = Clock.systemUTC(),
) {

    /** The clock in the account's zone, for java.time's `now(clock)` functions. */
    val clock: Clock = clock.withZone(zone)

    fun now(): ZonedDateTime = ZonedDateTime.now(clock)

    fun today(): LocalDate = LocalDate.now(clock)

    /** [instant] on the account's wall clock, for display. */
    fun local(instant: Instant): ZonedDateTime = instant.atZone(zone)

    /** The first day of the week that holds [date], as startOfWeek in ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts. */
    fun startOfWeek(date: LocalDate): LocalDate = date.with(TemporalAdjusters.previousOrSame(firstDayOfWeek))

    /**
     * The span the server will store for a stopped entry it has not received yet. This is
     * [snapUnsent][com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time.snapUnsent] in the account's zone with the
     * account's time resolution. Without a resolution the span comes back unchanged. Never pass
     * the running entry.
     */
    fun snapUnsent(start: Instant, stop: Instant): SnappedSpan =
        snapUnsent(start, stop, timeResolutionMinutes, zone)
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/ServerConfigStore.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session

import android.content.Context
import android.content.SharedPreferences
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
import java.io.IOException

/**
 * The configured server's base URL, in the app's private preferences. The URL
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
 *
 * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
 */
class ServerConfigStore(context: Context) {

    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }

    /** The stored server, or null when there is none or it no longer parses. */
    fun load(): ServerUrl? = preferences.getString(KEY_BASE_URL, null)?.let(ServerUrl::parse)

    @Throws(IOException::class)
    fun save(server: ServerUrl) {
        commit { putString(KEY_BASE_URL, server.toString()) }
    }

    @Throws(IOException::class)
    fun clear() {
        commit { remove(KEY_BASE_URL) }
    }

    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
    }

    internal companion object {
        const val FILE_NAME = "server_config"
        private const val KEY_BASE_URL = "base_url"
    }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/session/ServerTrustStore.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session

import android.content.Context
import android.content.SharedPreferences
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerCertificate
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
import java.io.IOException

/**
 * The user's trust decisions, in the app's private preferences file
 * `server_trust`, next to the server configuration: the one plain `http://`
 * server URL the user confirmed, and the one pinned certificate, as the TLS
 * server's host and port and the SHA-256 of its leaf certificate. A new
 * confirmation or pin replaces the old one, so another URL or certificate
[credential-related line omitted]
 * rules exclude it with every other file. Sign-out removes it with [clear].
 *
 * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
 */
class ServerTrustStore(context: Context) : ServerTrust {

    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }

    override fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean =
        server.isCleartext && preferences.getString(KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL, null) == server.toString()

    @Throws(IOException::class)
    override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) {
        commit { putString(KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL, server.toString()) }
    }

    override fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String? =
        preferences.takeIf { it.getString(KEY_PIN_HOST, null) == host && it.getInt(KEY_PIN_PORT, -1) == port }
            ?.getString(KEY_PIN_SHA256, null)

    @Throws(IOException::class)
    override fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate) {
        commit {
            putString(KEY_PIN_HOST, certificate.host)
            putInt(KEY_PIN_PORT, certificate.port)
            putString(KEY_PIN_SHA256, certificate.sha256)
        }
    }

    /** Forgets every decision, so each server asks again. */
    @Throws(IOException::class)
    fun clear() {
        commit { clear() }
    }

    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
    }

    internal companion object {
        const val FILE_NAME = "server_trust"
        private const val KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL = "cleartext_url"
        private const val KEY_PIN_HOST = "pin_host"
        private const val KEY_PIN_PORT = "pin_port"
        private const val KEY_PIN_SHA256 = "pin_sha256"
    }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/ConfiguredServer.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

/**
 * The one server the app may send requests to (docs/PRD.md principle 1).
 * [SharedHttpClient] refuses every request and every redirect whose scheme,
 * host or port differs from [current].
 */
object ConfiguredServer {

    @Volatile
    private var stored: () -> ServerUrl? = { null }

    @Volatile
    private var signInCandidate: ServerUrl? = null

    private val signInLock = Any()

    /**
     * During sign-in, the URL being tried. Otherwise the stored server. Null
     * when there is neither, which refuses every request.
     */
    val current: ServerUrl?
        get() = signInCandidate ?: stored()

    /** Where [current] reads the stored server from. The app sets this once at start. */
    fun readStoredFrom(source: () -> ServerUrl?) {
        stored = source
    }

    /**
     * Runs [attempt] with [candidate] as the configured server, since sign-in
     * talks to a server before the app has stored it. One attempt runs at a
     * time, and the stored server applies again when it returns.
     */
    fun <T> signingIn(candidate: ServerUrl, attempt: () -> T): T =
        synchronized(signInLock) {
            signInCandidate = candidate
            try {
                attempt()
            } finally {
                signInCandidate = null
            }
        }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/ServerTrust.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

import java.io.IOException

/**
 * What the user has agreed to for a server: sending to a plain `http://` URL,
 * and trusting a self-signed certificate by its fingerprint. [SharedHttpClient]
 * checks it on every request and every TLS handshake, so no screen or
 * background job can skip the question.
 */
interface ServerTrust {

    /**
     * Whether the user confirmed that requests to [server], a plain `http://`
[credential-related line omitted]
     */
    fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean

    /** Records the user's confirmation for [server]. Does disk I/O. */
    @Throws(IOException::class)
    fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl)

    /**
     * The SHA-256 of the certificate the user trusted for the TLS server at
     * [host] and [port], as lowercase hex, or null when there is none.
     */
    fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String?

    /** Trusts [certificate], and no other, for its host and port. Does disk I/O. */
    @Throws(IOException::class)
    fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate)
}

/**
 * Where [SharedHttpClient] reads the user's trust decisions from. The app sets
 * this once at start. Until then nothing is trusted, so every request to a
 * plain `http://` server is refused, and only certificates that a system or
 * user CA signed pass.
 */
object ConfiguredTrust {

    @Volatile
    var current: ServerTrust = TrustNothing
        private set

    fun readFrom(trust: ServerTrust) {
        current = trust
    }

    private object TrustNothing : ServerTrust {
        override fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean = false

        override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) {
            throw IOException("No trust store is configured")
        }

        override fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String? = null

        override fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate) {
            throw IOException("No trust store is configured")
        }
    }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/ConfiguredServerGuard.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

import java.io.IOException
import java.net.ProtocolException
import okhttp3.HttpUrl
import okhttp3.Interceptor
import okhttp3.Request
import okhttp3.Response

/**
 * The guard refused a request, or a redirect, to [origin] (`scheme://host:port`),
 * which is not the configured server. Nothing was sent there. The message
 * names the origin only: no path, query or credential.
 */
class HostNotAllowedException(val origin: String) :
    IOException("Refused a request to $origin, which is not the configured server")

/**
 * The guard refused a request to [server], a plain `http://` URL the user has
 * not confirmed, so nothing was sent. The message names the URL, which holds
 * no credential.
 */
class CleartextNotConfirmedException(val server: ServerUrl) :
    IOException("Refused a request to $server, a plain http server the user has not confirmed")

/**
 * Lets requests reach the configured server only. It checks the request
 * before OkHttp connects, and it follows redirects itself: OkHttp would
 * follow them inside the call, past any application interceptor, so
 * [SharedHttpClient] turns automatic redirects off. A redirect to the same
 * origin is followed, with OkHttp's rules for the method and body. A redirect
 * anywhere else fails with [HostNotAllowedException].
 *
 * A plain `http://` server gets no request until the user has confirmed its
[credential-related line omitted]
 * guard refuses it with [CleartextNotConfirmedException]. A redirect stays on
 * the same origin, and so on the same scheme.
 */
internal class ConfiguredServerGuard(
    private val configured: () -> ServerUrl?,
    private val trust: () -> ServerTrust,
) : Interceptor {

    override fun intercept(chain: Interceptor.Chain): Response {
        var request = chain.request()
        // One server for the whole call, redirects included.
        val server = requireAllowed(configured(), request.url)
        if (server.isCleartext && !trust().isCleartextConfirmed(server)) throw CleartextNotConfirmedException(server)
        var followUps = 0
        while (true) {
            val response = chain.proceed(request)
            val next = redirect(request, response) ?: return response
            response.close()
            requireAllowed(server, next.url)
            if (++followUps > MAX_FOLLOW_UPS) throw ProtocolException("Too many redirects: $followUps")
            request = next
        }
    }

    private fun requireAllowed(server: ServerUrl?, url: HttpUrl): ServerUrl {
        if (server == null || !server.isSameOrigin(url)) throw HostNotAllowedException(url.origin())
        return server
    }

    /** The request OkHttp would send for a redirect, or null when [response] is not one it follows. */
    private fun redirect(request: Request, response: Response): Request? {
        if (response.code !in REDIRECT_CODES) return null
        val location = response.header("Location") ?: return null
        val url = request.url.resolve(location) ?: return null
        val builder = request.newBuilder().url(url)
        val keepsMethod = response.code == HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT || response.code == HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT
        if (request.method != "GET" && request.method != "HEAD" && !keepsMethod) {
            builder.method("GET", null)
                .removeHeader("Transfer-Encoding")
                .removeHeader("Content-Length")
                .removeHeader("Content-Type")
        }
        return builder.build()
    }

    private companion object {
        const val HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT = 307
        const val HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT = 308
        val REDIRECT_CODES = setOf(300, 301, 302, 303, HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT, HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT)

        /** OkHttp's own limit. */
        const val MAX_FOLLOW_UPS = 20
    }
}

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/SharedHttpClient.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

import java.security.KeyStore
import javax.net.ssl.SSLContext
import javax.net.ssl.TrustManagerFactory
import javax.net.ssl.X509TrustManager
import kotlinx.serialization.json.Json
import okhttp3.OkHttpClient

/**
 * The app's only [OkHttpClient]. Every request goes through it, so its
 * connection pool, dispatcher and security settings apply everywhere.
 * SingleHttpClientTest fails when other app code builds an HTTP client.
 *
 * Every request and redirect must stay on [ConfiguredServer.current], and a
 * plain `http://` server needs the user's confirmation in
 * [ConfiguredTrust.current]; see [ConfiguredServerGuard]. TLS goes through
 * [PinningTrustManager]: the platform's CAs first, then the certificate the
 * user pinned. OkHttp's own host name check stays on. Later security
 * settings go into [build], never into a second client.
 */
object SharedHttpClient {

    val instance: OkHttpClient by lazy { build() }

    private fun build(): OkHttpClient {
        val trustManager = PinningTrustManager(platformTrustManager()) { host, port ->
            ConfiguredTrust.current.pinnedSha256(host, port)
        }
        val tls = SSLContext.getInstance("TLS").apply { init(null, arrayOf(trustManager), null) }
        return OkHttpClient.Builder()
            // The guard follows same-origin redirects itself.
            .followRedirects(false)
            .followSslRedirects(false)
            .sslSocketFactory(tls.socketFactory, trustManager)
            .addInterceptor(ConfiguredServerGuard(configured = { ConfiguredServer.current }, trust = { ConfiguredTrust.current }))
            .build()
    }

    /**
     * The platform's default trust manager. On Android it trusts the system
     * and user CAs that res/xml/network_security_config.xml names.
     */
    private fun platformTrustManager(): X509TrustManager =
        TrustManagerFactory.getInstance(TrustManagerFactory.getDefaultAlgorithm())
            .apply { init(null as KeyStore?) }
            .trustManagers
            .filterIsInstance<X509TrustManager>()
            .single()
}

/** JSON settings for both APIs. Models declare only the fields the app reads. */
internal val ApiJson: Json = Json { ignoreUnknownKeys = true }

app/src/test/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/time/NoDeviceZoneTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time

import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ClassReferences
import java.time.Clock
import java.time.LocalDate
import java.time.LocalDateTime
import java.time.ZoneId
import java.time.ZonedDateTime
import java.util.Calendar
import java.util.GregorianCalendar
import java.util.TimeZone
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The app shows and reads times in the account's zone only (docs/PRD.md S7), so no app class
 * reads the device's zone: no `ZoneId.systemDefault()`, no `TimeZone.getDefault()`, no `now()`
 * without a clock and no Calendar without a zone. Screens take all of it from [AccountTime].
 * The test reads the constant pool of every compiled app class through [ClassReferences].
 */
class NoDeviceZoneTest {

    @Test
    fun noAppClassReadsTheDeviceZone() {
        assertTrue("the scan found the app's classes", "com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/time/AccountTime" in ClassReferences.app)

        val offenders = ClassReferences.app
            .filterKeys { it !in ALLOWED }
            .flatMap { (name, references) -> references.members.filter(::readsDeviceZone).map { "$name uses $it" } }

        assertTrue(
            "Take the zone, the clock and the week from AccountSettings.time() instead:\n" + offenders.joinToString("\n"),
            offenders.isEmpty(),
        )
    }

    /** Shows the scan finds each way in, so the test above cannot pass by finding nothing. */
    @Test
    fun scanSeesEveryWayToReadTheDeviceZone() {
        val found = references(DeviceZoneReader::class.java).members
            .filter(::readsDeviceZone)
            .map { "${it.owner}.${it.name}" }
            .toSet()

        assertEquals(
            setOf(
                "java/time/ZoneId.systemDefault",
                "java/util/TimeZone.getDefault",
                "android/icu/util/TimeZone.getDefault",
                "java/time/Clock.systemDefaultZone",
                "java/time/LocalDate.now",
                "java/time/LocalDateTime.now",
                "java/time/ZonedDateTime.now",
                "java/util/Calendar.getInstance",
                "java/util/GregorianCalendar.<init>",
            ),
            found,
        )
    }

    /** A zone or a clock passed in is how [AccountTime] works, so the scan lets it through. */
    @Test
    fun scanLetsAGivenZoneOrClockThrough() {
        val members = references(GivenZoneReader::class.java).members

        assertTrue("the scan sees the calls", members.any { it.owner == "java/time/LocalDate" && it.name == "now" })
        assertEquals(emptyList<ClassReferences.Member>(), members.filter(::readsDeviceZone))
    }

    private fun references(type: Class<*>): ClassReferences.References =
        ClassReferences.read(type).getValue(type.name.replace('.', '/'))

    private fun readsDeviceZone(member: ClassReferences.Member): Boolean {
        val parameters = member.descriptor.substringAfter('(').substringBefore(')')
        return when (member.owner) {
            "java/time/ZoneId" -> member.name == "systemDefault"
            "java/util/TimeZone", "android/icu/util/TimeZone" -> member.name == "getDefault"
            "java/time/Clock" -> member.name == "systemDefaultZone"
            in ZONED_TYPES -> member.name == "now" && parameters.isEmpty()
            in CALENDARS -> member.name in setOf("getInstance", "<init>") && "TimeZone;" !in parameters
            else -> false
        }
    }

    private companion object {
        /**
         * App classes that deliberately read the device's zone. None does yet. A class added
         * here needs a comment that says why the account's zone will not do.
         */
        val ALLOWED: Set<String> = emptySet()

        /** The java.time types whose `now()` without arguments takes the device's zone. */
        val ZONED_TYPES = setOf(
            "java/time/LocalDate",
            "java/time/LocalDateTime",
            "java/time/LocalTime",
            "java/time/ZonedDateTime",
            "java/time/OffsetDateTime",
            "java/time/OffsetTime",
            "java/time/Year",
            "java/time/YearMonth",
            "java/time/MonthDay",
        )

        /** Calendars that take the device's zone unless they are given one. */
        val CALENDARS = setOf("java/util/Calendar", "java/util/GregorianCalendar", "android/icu/util/Calendar")
    }
}

/** Reads the device's zone in each way the scan looks for. Only its class file is read; nothing calls it. */
@Suppress("unused")
private object DeviceZoneReader {
    fun zoneId(): ZoneId = ZoneId.systemDefault()

    fun timeZone(): TimeZone = TimeZone.getDefault()

    fun icuTimeZone(): android.icu.util.TimeZone = android.icu.util.TimeZone.getDefault()

    fun clock(): Clock = Clock.systemDefaultZone()

    fun today(): LocalDate = LocalDate.now()

    fun localNow(): LocalDateTime = LocalDateTime.now()

    fun zonedNow(): ZonedDateTime = ZonedDateTime.now()

    fun calendar(): Calendar = Calendar.getInstance()

    fun gregorian(): Calendar = GregorianCalendar()
}

/** Takes the zone or the clock from its caller, as [AccountTime] does. Only its class file is read. */
@Suppress("unused")
private object GivenZoneReader {
    fun today(clock: Clock): LocalDate = LocalDate.now(clock)

    fun zonedNow(zone: ZoneId): ZonedDateTime = ZonedDateTime.now(zone)

    fun calendar(zone: TimeZone): Calendar = Calendar.getInstance(zone)

    fun zone(name: String): ZoneId = ZoneId.of(name)
}

app/src/test/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/time/AccountTimeTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time

import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.AccountSettings
import java.time.Clock
import java.time.DayOfWeek
import java.time.Instant
import java.time.LocalDate
import java.time.LocalDateTime
import java.time.ZoneId
import java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter
import java.util.TimeZone
import org.junit.After
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Before
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The phone sits in Kiritimati (UTC+14) and the account in Los Angeles or Kolkata. Every time
 * comes out in the account's zone.
 */
class AccountTimeTest {

    private val deviceZone = TimeZone.getDefault()

    @Before
    fun putThePhoneInAnotherZone() {
        TimeZone.setDefault(TimeZone.getTimeZone(DEVICE_ZONE))
    }

    @After
    fun restoreTheZone() {
        TimeZone.setDefault(deviceZone)
    }

    @Test
    fun showsTimesInTheAccountZoneAndNotTheDevicesZone() {
        assertEquals("the test's premise", ZoneId.of(DEVICE_ZONE), ZoneId.systemDefault())
        // 04:00 on Sunday 18 October in Los Angeles, and already 01:00 on Monday the 19th in
        // Kiritimati. The clock carries the device's zone, which the account time ignores.
        val instant = Instant.parse("2026-10-18T11:00:00Z")
        val settings = AccountSettings("america/los_angeles", DayOfWeek.MONDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = null)

        val time = settings.time(Clock.fixed(instant, ZoneId.of(DEVICE_ZONE)))!!

        assertEquals(ZoneId.of("America/Los_Angeles"), time.zone)
        assertEquals(LocalDateTime.of(2026, 10, 18, 4, 0), time.local(instant).toLocalDateTime())
        assertEquals("04:00", DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("HH:mm").format(time.local(instant)))
        assertEquals(LocalDateTime.of(2026, 10, 18, 4, 0), time.now().toLocalDateTime())
        assertEquals(LocalDate.of(2026, 10, 18), time.today())
        assertEquals(LocalDate.of(2026, 10, 18), LocalDate.now(time.clock))
        // The week that holds Sunday the 18th starts on Monday the 12th, not on the device's Monday.
        assertEquals(LocalDate.of(2026, 10, 12), time.startOfWeek(time.today()))
        assertEquals(ZoneId.of("America/Los_Angeles"), settings.time()!!.clock.zone)
    }

    @Test
    fun snapsUnsentEntriesOnTheAccountZonesGrid() {
        // An hourly grid in Kolkata (UTC+05:30) falls on the half hour in UTC and in Kiritimati
        // on the hour. 10:10Z is 15:40 in Kolkata, so the start goes to 16:00 there, 10:30Z.
        val start = Instant.parse("2026-10-18T10:10:00Z")
        val stop = Instant.parse("2026-10-18T10:50:00Z")
        val time = AccountSettings("Asia/Kolkata", DayOfWeek.MONDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = 60.0).time()!!

        val snapped = time.snapUnsent(start, stop)

        assertEquals(SnappedSpan(Instant.parse("2026-10-18T10:30:00Z"), Instant.parse("2026-10-18T11:30:00Z")), snapped)
    }

    @Test
    fun withoutATimeResolutionUnsentEntriesKeepTheirTimes() {
        val start = Instant.parse("2026-10-18T10:10:07.250Z")
        val stop = Instant.parse("2026-10-18T10:52:41.900Z")
        val time = AccountSettings("Asia/Kolkata", DayOfWeek.MONDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = null).time()!!

        assertEquals(SnappedSpan(start, stop), time.snapUnsent(start, stop))
    }

    @Test
    fun aTimezoneThePhoneCannotResolveGivesNoTimesAndNoFallback() {
        assertNull(AccountSettings("Mars/Olympus_Mons", DayOfWeek.MONDAY, null).time())
        assertNull(AccountSettings("GMT+5", DayOfWeek.MONDAY, null).time())
    }

    private companion object {
        const val DEVICE_ZONE = "Pacific/Kiritimati"
    }
}

app/src/test/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/trust/SessionTrustModelTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.trust

import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiCredentials
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ConfiguredServer
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ConfiguredTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.InMemoryServerTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerCertificate
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.SharedHttpClient
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.UntrustedCertificateException
import java.io.IOException
import java.security.cert.CertificateException
import javax.net.ssl.SSLHandshakeException
import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import mockwebserver3.MockResponse
import mockwebserver3.MockWebServer
import okhttp3.OkHttpClient
import org.junit.After
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Before
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * A signed-in session's requests against a MockWebServer, with the session's
 * client reporting to the model as the app's Session does.
 */
class SessionTrustModelTest {

    private val server = MockWebServer()
    private val trust = InMemoryServerTrust()
    private lateinit var url: ServerUrl
    private var retries = 0
    private lateinit var model: SessionTrustModel

    @Before
    fun setUp() {
        server.start()
        url = ServerUrl.parse(server.url("/").toString())!!
        // A stored http session whose confirmation is missing.
        ConfiguredServer.readStoredFrom { url }
        ConfiguredTrust.readFrom(trust)
        model = model(trust)
    }

    @After
    fun tearDown() {
        ConfiguredServer.readStoredFrom { null }
        ConfiguredTrust.readFrom(InMemoryServerTrust())
        server.close()
    }

    @Test
    fun unconfirmedHttpSessionPromptsAndSendsNothingUntilTheUserConfirms() {
        server.enqueue(me())
        val client = client()

        val refused = client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        assertEquals(ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url)), refused)
        assertEquals("no request before the confirmation", 0, server.requestCount)
        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url), promptOpen = true), model.state.value)

        model.accept()

        assertTrue(trust.isCleartextConfirmed(url))
        assertEquals(1, retries)
        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(), model.state.value)
        assertTrue(client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer()) is ApiResult.Success)
[credential-related line omitted]
    }

    @Test
    fun cancellingKeepsRequestsRefusedAndTheSameRefusalLeavesThePromptClosed() {
        val client = client()
        client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        model.cancel()
        val again = client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        assertEquals(ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url)), again)
        assertEquals(0, server.requestCount)
        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url), promptOpen = false), model.state.value)

        model.reopen()

        assertTrue(model.state.value.promptOpen)
    }

    @Test
    fun changedCertificatePromptsWithTheNewOneAndAcceptingPinsIt() {
        trust.confirmCleartext(url)
        val old = CERTIFICATE.copy(sha256 = "cd".repeat(32))
        trust.pinCertificate(old)
        server.enqueue(me())
        val client = client(handshakeAsIfServing(CERTIFICATE))

        val refused = client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        val refusal = ApiError.UntrustedCertificate(CERTIFICATE, changed = true)
        assertEquals(ApiResult.Failure(refusal), refused)
        assertEquals(0, server.requestCount)
        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(refusal, promptOpen = true), model.state.value)

        model.accept()

        assertEquals(CERTIFICATE.sha256, trust.pinnedSha256(CERTIFICATE.host, CERTIFICATE.port))
        assertTrue(client.get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer()) is ApiResult.Success)
        assertEquals(1, server.requestCount)
    }

    @Test
    fun anotherRefusalOpensThePromptAgain() {
        model.refused(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url))
        model.cancel()

        model.refused(ApiError.UntrustedCertificate(CERTIFICATE, changed = false))

        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(ApiError.UntrustedCertificate(CERTIFICATE, changed = false), promptOpen = true), model.state.value)
    }

    @Test
    fun otherNetworkFailuresShowNoPrompt() {
        trust.confirmCleartext(url)
        server.close()

        val result = client().get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        assertTrue(result.toString(), (result as ApiResult.Failure).error is ApiError.Network)
        assertNull(model.state.value.refusal)
    }

    @Test
    fun anAnswerFromTheServerClearsTheRefusal() {
        model.refused(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url))
        model.cancel()
        trust.confirmCleartext(url)
        server.enqueue(MockResponse.Builder().code(403).build())

        client().get(url.api("me"), JsonObject.serializer())

        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(), model.state.value)
    }

    @Test
    fun aFailedWriteKeepsTheRefusalAndTheWayBack() {
        val failing = object : ServerTrust by trust {
            override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) = throw IOException("disk full")
        }
        model = model(failing)
        model.refused(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url))

        model.accept()

        assertEquals(SessionTrustModel.State(ApiError.CleartextNotConfirmed(url), promptOpen = false), model.state.value)
        assertEquals(0, retries)
    }

    private fun model(trust: ServerTrust) =
        SessionTrustModel(trust, retry = { retries++ }, scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Unconfined), io = Dispatchers.Unconfined)

    /** The signed-in session's client, as Session builds it. */
    private fun client(httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance) =
[credential-related line omitted]

    /**
     * The shared client with a step that stands in for its trust manager: the
     * handshake with a server presenting [certificate] fails unless the user
     * pinned it, as PinningTrustManager makes it fail.
     */
    private fun handshakeAsIfServing(certificate: ServerCertificate): OkHttpClient =
        SharedHttpClient.instance.newBuilder()
            .addInterceptor { chain ->
                val pin = trust.pinnedSha256(certificate.host, certificate.port)
                if (pin != certificate.sha256) {
                    val refusal = UntrustedCertificateException(certificate, pinChanged = pin != null, cause = CertificateException("no trust anchor"))
                    throw SSLHandshakeException("handshake failed").apply { initCause(refusal) }
                }
                chain.proceed(chain.request())
            }
            .build()

    private fun me() = MockResponse.Builder()
        .code(200)
        .addHeader("Content-Type", "application/json")
        .body("""{"id":7}""")
        .build()

    private companion object {
        const val TOKEN = "[32-hex value omitted]"
        val CERTIFICATE = ServerCertificate("tokkl.test", 443, "ab".repeat(32), "CN=tokkl.test")
    }
}

app/src/test/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInModelTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin

import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiCredentials
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ConfiguredTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.InMemoryServerTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerCertificate
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerTrust
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.SharedHttpClient
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.UntrustedCertificateException
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.untrustedCertificate
import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.AccountSettings
import java.io.IOException
import java.time.DayOfWeek
import java.security.cert.CertificateException
import javax.net.ssl.SSLHandshakeException
import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import mockwebserver3.MockResponse
import mockwebserver3.MockWebServer
import okhttp3.OkHttpClient
import org.junit.After
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Before
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The sign-in form against a MockWebServer. The Tokkl container in the
 * instrumented tests serves at the root and must not lock its account, so the
 * base path and 429 cases live here.
 */
class SignInModelTest {

    private val server = MockWebServer()
    private val saved = mutableListOf<Pair<ServerUrl, String>>()
    private val savedSettings = mutableListOf<AccountSettings>()
    private var saveFailure: IOException? = null
    private var now = 5_000_000L
    private val cooldowns = InMemoryCooldowns()
    private lateinit var trust: InMemoryServerTrust
    private lateinit var model: SignInModel

    @Before
    fun setUp() {
        server.start()
        // Most tests sign in to the MockWebServer's plain http URLs, which the user has confirmed here.
        trust = InMemoryServerTrust(ServerUrl.parse(baseUrl("/"))!!, ServerUrl.parse(baseUrl("/tokkl"))!!)
        ConfiguredTrust.readFrom(trust)
        model = model(trust)
    }

    @After
    fun tearDown() {
        ConfiguredTrust.readFrom(InMemoryServerTrust())
        server.close()
    }

    private fun model(trust: ServerTrust, httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance): SignInModel {
        val signIn = SignIn(
            session = { url, token, settings ->
                saveFailure?.let { throw it }
                saved += url to token
                savedSettings += settings
            },
            httpClient = httpClient,
        )
        // Unconfined runs each attempt to the end inside submit().
        return SignInModel(signIn, trust, cooldowns, CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Unconfined), Dispatchers.Unconfined, clock = { now })
    }

    /** The deadlines a [SignInCooldownStore] would hold, in memory. */
    private class InMemoryCooldowns : SignInCooldowns {
        val stored = mutableMapOf<String, Long>()

        override fun load(): Map<String, Long> = stored.toMap()

        override fun save(origin: String, deadline: Long) {
            stored[origin] = deadline
        }

        override fun clear(origin: String) {
            stored.remove(origin)
        }
    }

    @Test
    fun tooManyRequestsBlocksSubmitUntilRetryAfterPassesAndNeverRetriesAlone() {

[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]

        model.submit()

        assertEquals(SignInResult.RateLimited(30), model.state.value.failure)
        assertEquals(30, model.secondsUntilRetry())
        now += 29_001
        assertEquals(1, model.secondsUntilRetry())
        model.submit()
        assertEquals("submit is refused while the countdown runs", 1, server.requestCount)

        now += 999
        assertEquals(0, model.secondsUntilRetry())
        assertEquals("nothing retries when the countdown ends", 1, server.requestCount)
        assertFalse(model.state.value.signedIn)

        model.submit()
        assertEquals(2, server.requestCount)

    @Test
    fun handshakeFailureFromThePinningTrustManagerCarriesTheCertificate() {
        val refusal = UntrustedCertificateException(CERTIFICATE, pinChanged = false, cause = CertificateException("no trust anchor"))
        val untrusted = SSLHandshakeException("handshake failed").apply { initCause(refusal) }
        val platformOnly = SSLHandshakeException("handshake failed").apply {
            initCause(CertificateException("Trust anchor for certification path not found."))
        }

        assertEquals(refusal, untrusted.untrustedCertificate())
        assertNull(platformOnly.untrustedCertificate())
        assertNull(IOException("refused").untrustedCertificate())
    }

    @Test
    fun untrustedCertificatePromptsAndTrustingItPinsThatCertificateThenSends() {
[credential-related line omitted]
        model = model(trust, handshakeAsIfServing(CERTIFICATE))
[credential-related line omitted]

        model.submit()

        assertEquals(SignInResult.UntrustedCertificate(CERTIFICATE, changed = false), model.state.value.failure)
        assertEquals("no request before the user trusts the certificate", 0, server.requestCount)

        model.trustCertificate()

        assertEquals(mapOf("${CERTIFICATE.host}:${CERTIFICATE.port}" to CERTIFICATE.sha256), trust.pins)
        assertEquals(1, server.requestCount)
        assertTrue(model.state.value.signedIn)
    }

    @Test
    fun changedCertificatePromptsAgainAndCancelKeepsTheOldPin() {
        val old = "cd".repeat(32)
        trust.pins["${CERTIFICATE.host}:${CERTIFICATE.port}"] = old
        model = model(trust, handshakeAsIfServing(CERTIFICATE))
[credential-related line omitted]

        model.submit()
        assertEquals(SignInResult.UntrustedCertificate(CERTIFICATE, changed = true), model.state.value.failure)

        model.dismissPrompt()

        assertNull(model.state.value.failure)
        assertEquals(old, trust.pinnedSha256(CERTIFICATE.host, CERTIFICATE.port))
        assertEquals(0, server.requestCount)
    }

    /**
     * The shared client with a step that stands in for its trust manager: the
     * handshake with a server presenting [certificate] fails unless the user
     * pinned it, as PinningTrustManager makes it fail.
     */
    private fun handshakeAsIfServing(certificate: ServerCertificate): OkHttpClient =
        SharedHttpClient.instance.newBuilder()
            .addInterceptor { chain ->
                val pin = trust.pinnedSha256(certificate.host, certificate.port)
                if (pin != certificate.sha256) {
                    val refusal = UntrustedCertificateException(certificate, pinChanged = pin != null, cause = CertificateException("no trust anchor"))
                    throw SSLHandshakeException("handshake failed").apply { initCause(refusal) }
                }
                chain.proceed(chain.request())
            }
            .build()

    @Test
    fun plainHttpServerShowsTheWarningAndSendsNothingUntilTheUserConfirms() {

[credential-related line omitted]

        model.submit()

        assertFalse(model.state.value.submitting)
        assertEquals("no request before the confirmation", 0, server.requestCount)

        model.confirmCleartext()

        assertTrue(trust.isCleartextConfirmed(fresh))
        assertEquals(1, server.requestCount)
        assertTrue(model.state.value.signedIn)
    }

    }
